# FSM Platform v1 — Issue Index

Tracer-bullet vertical slices for the greenfield FSM GPS Field Service Management platform
(NestJS backend + React/Vite admin dashboard + RN/Expo SE mobile app). Each issue is a thin
end-to-end slice (schema → API → UI → tests). Generated by `/to-issues` from `docs/`
(priority: PRD-fsm-admin-dashboard.md → fsm-business-technical-workflow.md → backend design docs).

Triage: AFK slices are `ready-for-agent`; HITL slices (01, 03) are `ready-for-human`.
Sequence loosely follows the backend LLD phases P0–P7. "Blocked by" gives the build order.

> **Branch lineage (historical):** on 2026-06-29 the two divergent lines (`feat/fe-enterprise-ui`,
> `feat/issue-34-install-lifecycle`) were merged into `integration/fe-plus-backend` (merge `fd64150`;
> the `#71`/`#72` collision was resolved by renumbering to **`#79`/`#80`** — see
> [`../../docs/archive/HANDOFF-2026-06-29-integration.md`](../../docs/archive/HANDOFF-2026-06-29-integration.md)).
> Since then all work continued on **`feat/autoplant-integration`** (pushed to origin 2026-07-10),
> which is the current single line of development — see `docs/SYSTEM-STATE-2026-07.md` §8.

> **Convention — check status lines against `git log`/source before trusting them.** This tracker
> and the docs it links have gone stale-in-place four times: #99 (row 2, this file) and #176 marked
> done while a doc elsewhere still described them as open; the 2026-07-31 implementation audit's
> #162 line went stale three days later when #162 actually landed; #91's own "still live" status
> line undercounted how much of its schema had already shipped (`dd9846b`, Slice 1). None of these
> were wrong when written — the doc body just wasn't edited in place when reality moved past it,
> which is exactly the drift this file's own "edit in place, never fork" rule exists to prevent. A
> status line (`Status:`, `Progress:`, a row's Evidence column, a "done"/"still live" claim in prose)
> is a **hypothesis to re-check against `git log`/the current source**, not a fact to carry forward —
> especially for anything security- or auth-relevant, where a few days' staleness has already produced
> a materially wrong risk read twice (see `docs/status/mobile-readiness-2026-08.md` Phase 3).

> **RESEQUENCED 2026-07-22 (end of session) — #153 BEFORE #146.** #146's slice-1 acceptance
> criterion ("a deferred ticket leaves the day plan, the rest of the plan is untouched") and its
> slice-2 capacity criterion are **both unobservable** until #153 lands: the day plan is already

### Mobile contract-freeze block (filed 2026-07-28 — `docs/status/mobile-backend-freeze-plan-2026-07-28.md`)

Three mobile-readiness assessments reconciled into one plan against a **stricter bar**: the backend
contract must be frozen *before* mobile starts, so that no backend change is needed mid-build. The
plan supersedes all three source documents (banners added). **Honest verdict on the bar: ~85%
achievable — four categories (field-level completeness, client-discovered error cases, offline sync
semantics, real-device performance) cannot be frozen ahead of a client.** The recommended bar is
contract-*compatibility* (versioning + additive-only + shared types + OTA) rather than
contract-*immutability*: **59–87 engineer-days vs 96–142**. §4 of the plan is the deliverable — a
34-route contract inventory with per-route STABLE/CHANGE/NEW/DECIDE verdicts, 54 error codes, and
the serialization hazards.

Six new issues (**#169–#174**); extensions appended to **#161–#167** (revised) and
**#107 #91 #84 #81 #59 #88** (plus the earlier #101/#106/#110/#147/#111/#82/#76 extensions,
corrected).

> [#168 admin density + dark mode](./issues/168-admin-density-dark-mode.md) (done, session log below).
> Renumbered to **[#174](./issues/174-se-request-validation-dtos.md)** per the `#71`/`#72` → `#79`/`#80`
> precedent in the branch-lineage note; all cross-references updated. #168 keeps the admin work.

### Mobile pilot-readiness block (filed 2026-08-04 — `audit/mobile-device-readiness-2026-08-04.md` + `audit/mobile-contract-sync-audit-2026-08-04.md`)

**This block has a parent issue, unlike the two above** —
[**#197**](./issues/197-mobile-pilot-readiness-remediation-epic.md) is the pilot go/no-go owner, which
is what [#170](./issues/170-mobile-release-upgrade-mechanism.md)'s *"owner needed before pilot"* has
lacked since 2026-08-03. Read the epic first; it carries the execution order, the coverage matrix
(every audit finding ID → slice or explicit disposition), and the in-place corrections.

Four structural root causes, not a list of bugs: **(1)** mobile has no HTTP middleware layer, so every
screen hand-rolls auth and maps any failure to "Offline"; **(2)** contract types are synchronised by
convention, not types — no parity test exists anywhere; **(3)** the admin app does not consume
contracts that already ship (`GET /api/media/:id`, `/api/intraday-insertions`, the whole notification
spine — **zero** callers each), so three PRD-specified manager workflows are dead on a green backend;
**(4)** no document defines what "today" means, while three surfaces each assume a different answer.
Underneath all four: no native build has ever been produced and the one SE who can log in has no work
data.

**Decisions — ALL THREE RULED 2026-08-04, so no slice in this block is decision-blocked:**
[#198](./issues/198-decision-day-boundary-and-dispatch-clock.md) → the operating day is the **IST day**
(`Asia/Kolkata`); dispatch at **05:00 IST** default, `Asia/Kolkata` business timezone, **operator-configurable
not hardcoded**; leave dates are IST calendar days ·
[#199](./issues/199-decision-one-active-device-cross-surface.md) → **one handset + one browser** (a second
*handset* login still revokes the first), plus a distinct "signed in on another device" error code and a
short rotation grace window ·
[#200](./issues/200-decision-deferred-vs-removed-presentation.md) → day-plan changes are
**server-authoritative and restart-durable**, defer shows its return date, and a bulk rebalance gets its own
"plan being rebuilt" state.

The dispatch ruling was wider than the question, so it spawned
[**#213**](./issues/213-configurable-dispatch-schedule.md) — but **four of the five asks already exist**
(manual `POST /schedules/dispatch-run`, its OH/CSM role gate, its full MANUAL+actor audit bracket, and its
admin button). Only two gaps are real: the schedule is an env var rather than a `system_settings` entry, and
the single-in-flight guard sits on the scheduler while the manual trigger calls the run service directly —
so the path an operator uses in an emergency is the one with no concurrency guard.

Building #204 surfaced a second ruling the audits had missed entirely: the **analytics** day was still
UTC while operations moved to IST, so a ticket closed at 02:00 IST lands on today's Day Plan but in
yesterday's efficiency cube. Ruled 2026-08-04 — **move analytics to the IST day and recompute**,
accepting that recompute rewrites recorded rows (CONTEXT.md §19 addendum). Filed as
[**#214**](./issues/214-analytics-day-ist-recompute.md), which carries the part #204 could not answer:
four of the five cubes were never audited, and #214 requires a written finding on each *before*
anything is recomputed.

**Slices, in execution order** — P0 can't-test-anything: [#209](./issues/209-mobile-build-pipeline-device-install.md) build pipeline ·
[#210](./issues/210-dev-dataset-loginable-se-with-work.md) dev dataset. P1 app-misreports-state:
**#186** (extended in place) · [#201](./issues/201-mobile-consumes-server-day-plan-signal.md) ·
[#202](./issues/202-cross-surface-session-semantics.md) · [#204](./issues/204-time-semantics-day-boundary-implementation.md) **✅ DONE 2026-08-04**.
P2 dead-workflow: [#203](./issues/203-admin-media-display.md) · [#206](./issues/206-admin-manager-action-surface.md).
P3 correctness: **#169** items 1-2 + **#174** · [#205](./issues/205-contract-typing-shared-enums.md) ·
[#207](./issues/207-admin-queue-filter-availability-completeness.md) · [#208](./issues/208-label-vocabulary-parity.md).
P4 hygiene: [#211](./issues/211-status-truth-doc-hygiene.md) · [#212](./issues/212-admin-repoint-api-v1.md).
Filed after the epic, both off #204's rulings: [#213](./issues/213-configurable-dispatch-schedule.md)
(dispatch schedule config + overlap guard) · [#214](./issues/214-analytics-day-ist-recompute.md)
(analytics day + recompute) — #214 blocks nothing but grows more expensive every day it waits, because
every day writes more rows on the old boundary.

### Suite-repair block (filed 2026-07-31 — the backend suite is not currently a measurement instrument)

Four runs on the **same commit** (`786b080`, clean tree) produced four different results: run 1 (flag
off) 7 files / 11 tests failed / 1273 passed / 11 skipped · run 2 (flag off) 7 / 11 / 1266 / 11 ·
run 3 (flag on) 9 / 10 / 1270 / 15 · `docs/audits/2026-07-31-implementation-audit.md` (flag on)
7 / 8 / 1271 / 11. Admin (89 files / 386 tests) and typecheck (4/4) are stable. **Where the audit
disagrees with those measured runs, the measured runs win.**

> **AMENDED 2026-07-31 — the headline cause was misattributed, and [#184](./issues/184-vitest-worker-exited-unexpectedly.md)
> was filed for it.** Decoding the raw logs (`baseline-failures.txt` / `baseline-flag-on.txt`, UTF-16LE)
> shows **all three runs collected the identical universe — `(315)` files, `(1295)` tests.** Runs 1 and 3
> account for all 1295; **run 2 accounts for 1288 and reports `Errors 2 errors`**. The two files that
> vanished are `settings-write.e2e-spec.ts` (3 tests) and `plant-zone-change-impact.e2e-spec.ts`
> (4 tests) — **7 tests, exactly the 1273 → 1266 delta.** So the run-1-vs-run-2 variance is **entirely**
> the worker crash, not database state. `settings-write` is a repeat offender, named in
> [#156](./issues/156-test-db-orphan-accumulation.md):45 on 2026-07-22 across a full manual truncate —
> a clean DB does not fix it. #180's orphan work stands on its own, but **#180 AC-1 and #184 AC-6 are
> one gate and neither is reachable alone.**

**Build order is mandatory and is restated inside each file: #180 (+#184 alongside) → #181 + #182
together → #183.** Until #180 lands, no other fix is verifiable. #181 and #182 are **coupled through
one mechanism** —
with the flag off, ticks short-circuit to `DISABLED` before touching collaborators and *mask* #181's
constructor bug, so **landing #182 alone makes the suite look worse** as #181's failures surface.
The implementer must be warned or it will think it broke something.

`BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED` stays `"true"` in `apps/backend/.env:41` for the whole repair programme so
the specs are measured against the env they were written under; returning it to `"false"` before any
dev server runs is audit R6 / [#148](./issues/148-verification-stale-telemetry.md), not this block.

Root cause (AC-3): a Windows-native per-forked-child fault (5/6 reproduced crashes = NTSTATUS `0xC0000409` `STATUS_STACK_BUFFER_OVERRUN`, 1/6 = `0xC0000142` `STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED`), file-content-independent (hit a non-`AppModule` file) and not reduced by fewer forks (`singleFork` tested, rejected — same crash rate, catastrophically worse blast radius). Not fixable in `src/`/`test/` logic, so AC-4 ships auto-recovery instead: `scripts/run-tests.mjs` detects exactly which file(s) a crash dropped and retries only those — validated against the same 3-full-suite-run count as the baseline (2 of 3 crashed, both fully recovered, 317/317 both times). `settings-write` (AC-7) and `plant-zone-change-impact` (AC-8, H3) both cleared as non-causal with 20-loop evidence | S/M — **co-gate with #180 AC-1 (met)**; now fully closed |

The operator ruled the eight open scheduler decisions (scoring, capacity, CRITICAL, eligibility,
filters, distance, VU/SLA, concurrency/deployment) plus guarantees G1–G8, then — after the
pre-implementation review — the two questions that review surfaced: **Q-A** (the Plant Cluster
Multiplier becomes candidate-specific, because it could not otherwise influence any decision) and

self-authorises an overload). #258 is the record; the table below is the **corrected** build order.

now hard prerequisites): **#177** before #266 (a selection rewrite belongs over a correct pool) and
**#178** before #269/#268 (capacity must be honest before it is displayed or escalated on).

and close #252 there.

## P9 — Assign Work Console (approved UI direction [#272](./issues/272-decision-assign-work-console.md), approved 2026-08-20)

The operator approved a single manual-assignment console to replace the seven scattered surfaces that
can move work to an engineer today — none of which shows how much work exists, how much is left after
the write, or whether the chosen engineer can carry it. **#272 is the record; the design file is
authoritative** and lives in the repo at
[`docs/ui/desktop/approved-designs/assign-work-console.html`](../../docs/ui/desktop/approved-designs/assign-work-console.html)
(published copy: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/369ddf01-b727-4d9b-b086-00714480bb74). No v2
reference image covers this screen; approved designs now sit at the same authority level as the v2
set for screens the reference never drew — see `docs/agents/domain.md` § UI authority and the
`approved-designs/README.md` index. **Do not redesign the console; build it.**

**What was wrong, in one line:** every manual write in the codebase is shaped N→1 (many tickets, one
engineer, committed immediately, no preview, no residual) while the dispatcher's job is M→N.

**P9 depends on P8 and cannot precede it.** The console displays capacity and candidate order, so its
numbers must be true before they are shown: **#178** (closure clears assignment, or the load column
lies) → **#269** (the one `{committed, dailyCapacity}` payload — P9 consumes it and defines no second
counter) · **#266** (score decides nothing today at `recommender.service.ts:466`, so a rendered
candidate order would teach a false model) · **#262** (the per-SE transaction shape `assign-batch`
must match) · **#265** (clean 409s — the console multiplies concurrent manual writes) · **#250** ✅
(the dry-run seam Distribute projects through).

**#269 stays "existing pages, no redesign" and is not superseded** — it is the instrumentation this
console was written to consume.

**Three operator questions are open and non-blocking** (each answered before the issue that needs it,
listed in #272): Device Detail panel retired or kept as a deep-link shortcut (before #277) · draft
persistence, session-local recommended (before #273's draft model) · whether ZM gets Distribute or
only OH + CSM (before #276).

Build order — **strictly sequential**; each slice is usable on its own:

## Deferred / decided-against

Decisions to **not** build, with the analysis that settled them. Unlike Next-up, nothing here is
scheduled — items move out only on new evidence.

- **Per-zone engine *configuration* (raw ZM-editable knobs) — DECIDED AGAINST (2026-07-21).**
  Analysis: [`docs/proposals/zone-engine-customization-2026-07-21.md`](../../docs/proposals/zone-engine-customization-2026-07-21.md)
  (commit `f0f3dcb`). Exposing ticket-creation / assignment-engine settings as per-zone knobs a ZM
  edits is a net-negative: the most-wanted knob (`inactivity_threshold_hours`) defines the Fleet-Uptime
  / Soft-Inactive **denominator**, so per-zone values make zones non-comparable in the very reports
  that grade them **and** let a graded ZM tune their own scorecard; `eligibility_mode` and scoring
  weights fail the same test; and it taxes the single-scalar set-based recompute hot path. Most real ZM
  wants already exist as per-instance levers (override / same-day / planner / capacity). **What we build
  instead:** legibility now → [#136](./issues/136-zone-operating-mode-visibility.md); two OH-bounded
  postures only on evidenced demand → [#137](./issues/137-per-zone-operating-postures.md) (deferred).
- **Per-zone operating postures (ZM-tunable, OH-bounded) → [#137](./issues/137-per-zone-operating-postures.md)**
  — deferred, gated on evidenced operator demand + OH sign-off (proposal §4 Approach B / §5 P2). Not
  scheduled; filed so the option isn't lost.

| 2026-07-16 | **Company/Plant Overview device tickets — "Batch" column + batches made addressable by id.** Column sits beside Assignment: a batched ticket renders `Batch #<id> →`, an unbatched one a non-clickable `—`; the link `stopPropagation`s so it doesn't also open the ticket drawer. Backend `ticket-query.service` LATERAL also selects `ws.run_id` → `runId` on `TicketView`/`TicketRow` (run context for the drill-down). **Root cause found by diagnosing an operator "clicking does nothing" report — the link wasn't the bug, the addressing scheme was.** Batch detail was only reachable as `/dispatch-runs/:runId/batches/:batchId`, but `work_schedules.run_id` is nullable (**pre-ledger + ZM_MANUAL**); measured on the dev DB, **1,235 tickets / 84 batches have no run_id vs 503 / 36 that do**, and `/schedules/:seId` is no better (it serves only the SE's *latest* schedule → 517/1,738), leaving **1,171 of 1,738 batched tickets (67%) reachable by neither route**. Fix: a batch is now addressed **by its own id** — `getBatchDetail(batchId, scope)` derives the run from the schedule (`DispatchBatchDetail.runId` → `string \| null`), ZM clamp moved onto `schedule.zoneId` (proven still to bite on run-less batches); new `GET /api/batches/:batchId` on BatchesController; the run-scoped API route + FE route are **removed** in favour of `/batches/:batchId` (zone drill-down + Batch column both link there). A run-less batch renders fully, minus the run-keyed trace and the zone breadcrumb (→ runs list). **Verified on real data: batches 5/6/7 (DEPOT_MCU/RCM/BANGALORE-BIDADI-DPG) resolve with their tickets where they 404'd before; foreign-zone read still 404s; linkable batched tickets 503 → 1,738.** New tests: backend e2e run-less batch + ZM clamp on run-less batch (12/12), FE run-less render + link-without-run. #99 route sweep green (new route 401s bare). Both apps tsc clean; admin suite 268 pass — the 1 failure (`dashboard-role-variants`, "auto-dispatch system efficiency") is **not from this work**: an unrelated in-flight edit to `OpsHeadDashboard.tsx` removed that row without updating its test. | uncommitted |

| 2026-07-17 | **AutoPlant enrichment surfaced (Inactive Duration · IMSI No · Device Type · Trip Creation) on `/reports/device` + `/batches/:batchId`.** Analysed the funnel end-to-end first, then split the fields by **measured lifecycle, not by name**: `DEVICE_TYPE` is static (91 of 24,173 devices ever changed it across 922k pings = **0.38%**) and `IMSI_NO` is SIM identity → **master sync**; `TRIP_CREATION_DATETIME` is **live trip state** (**2,614/14,205 DEPLOYED = 18.4%/day**, tracks `active_trip_id`; vs 0.2%/day for the genuine fitment attr `device_installation_date`) → **snapshot tick**. **Root-caused `devices.device_type` = NULL fleet-wide (0 of 20,935)**: `master-mapping.ts` mirrored it, but `autoplant-master-source.ts` fed it `NULL AS device_type`, so every sync wrote NULL — fixed with `LEFT JOIN ap_widgets.tb_vehiclemaster ON vehicle_no` (fan-out-safe: `vehicle_no` is PK, 60,601/60,601 distinct, 100% DEPLOYED match; **zero extra queries**, <100-row DBA cap intact) + new `devices.imsi_no`. Trip creation rides the existing reader SELECT → `device_states.trip_creation_datetime` through the SAME `unnest`+`GREATEST` upsert (no new write path; a NULL never clears a stored trip). **Timezone trap caught**: `latest_gps_datetime` is a naive DATETIME in IST (+330), but `TRIP_CREATION_DATETIME` is a **TIMESTAMP** the server returns in the session zone (**UTC**) → normalized at offset **0**; the IST path would have shifted every trip stamp 5.5h. A widgets outage now FAILS the master run rather than degrading it (a NULL-substituting run would wipe device_type/imsi fleet-wide; a failed run writes nothing). Inactive Duration **reused** (`device_states.latest_gps_datetime` + existing `formatInactiveDuration`) — no new calculation, and it already existed on `/reports/device`. Migration `20260717120000_autoplant_device_enrichment` (2 additive nullable columns). **Verified live against prod AutoPlant**: the enrichment join returns 70/90 device_type + 59/90 imsi on page 1; the reader returns 86/90 trip stamps, newest 28 min old (a wrong +330 would read ~6h stale). **backend 1063 pass / 0 fail** (+16 new: trip-creation TZ specs, master-source join specs, `snapshot-trip-creation-dualwrite.e2e-spec.ts`), **admin device-detail + batch-detail 19/19**, tsc clean both apps. Stale doc claims corrected in SYSTEM-STATE §2.2/§3a/§3b — including the `ap_widgets` "grant pending / does not exist" comments in `autoplant-mysql.client.ts` + `autoplant-ping.ts`, disproven by a live read (telemetry fresh to today). **Pre-existing, NOT from this work**: admin `test/dashboard-role-variants.test.tsx` fails — the uncommitted OpsHead re-theme removed the Auto-Dispatch Efficiency row that the test still asserts. | uncommitted |

Quantified with bounded prod SELECTs (≤90 rows/query): FSM 20,856 vehicles ALL 'DEPLOYED' vs AP 15,652 deployed; 90-device sample → 33% UNDEPLOYED + 3% vanished (~37% stale, ≥5,204 floor); **live dispatch batches checked exhaustively: 739/1,738 (42.5%) UNDEPLOYED + 118 absent ≈ half the dispatch workload**; inactive population 58% departed (34+18 of 90); ~3,700/18,188 OPEN tickets on departed devices; `eligibility_mode='all-deployed'` gate a no-op (20,925/20,925 eligible — mirror holds only 'DEPLOYED'); batch 59 / RR_D20710475 verified (OPEN ticket, AUTO_ASSIGNED, UNDEPLOYED at source). Reconciled: ingest WARNs = 8,336 pinging UNDEPLOYED devices never mirrored; the ~1,200 tb dup-device_id note is **stale (0 today)**. Both source status columns agree ~98.7% on UNDEPLOYED; tb labels ~half of mst's DEPLOYED as ACTIVE. Design proposal (#119 posture: observe/never-delete/reversible/audited): `device_departures` side table + `device_states.is_departed`; options A (widen read, recommended, +~365 q/day) / B (recon pass — the R10 §3 gap) / C (absence diff) + MISSING_FROM_SOURCE safety net with % guardrail; departed ⇒ ineligible both modes, excluded from inactive/SLA, recommender hard-filter drop, ZM/OH departed tally, auto-restore on re-deploy; dry-run-gated backfill. Deliverables: `docs/audits/deployment-lifecycle-investigation-2026-07-17.md`, issue **#128**, addendum line in the 07-16 risk audit. **Decisions taken same day**: Option A approved **with the insert scope pinned — widening the READ must not widen the CREATE** (FSM keeps creating only DEPLOYED/ACTIVE; never-deployed devices stay out, or `vehicles` balloons ~21k→~48k and every dashboard total changes meaning). That pin **overrides this session's own §2a draft**, which had proposed a whole-fleet registry to also silence the ingest WARNs — the WARNs stay, tracked separately as log hygiene. Tickets: cancel `DEVICE_UNDEPLOYED` (#119 semantics). Backfill: dry-run, operator approves counts, then apply; before/after captured in SYSTEM-STATE. | 2026-07-20 | **#130 add-ups ruled (3 operator proposals, authority delegated) — design amended, still docs-only.** (1) Status-authority: **documented as a binding three-tier hierarchy** (`device_departures` = lifecycle truth for safety gates → `vehicles.status` = verbatim last-observed AutoPlant mirror → `is_departed` = derived fast filter) — but the proposal's specifics corrected: **no enum widening** (`vehicles.status` is TEXT, schema.prisma:1660, and post-#128 already updates verbatim for known vehicles, `master-sync.service.ts:256-257`; an enum would make a novel AutoPlant value crash the sync instead of being observed as non-operational via the `OPERATIONAL_DEPLOYMENT_STATUSES` allow-list); **no `NEVER_DEPLOYED` value** (never-deployed devices have no FSM row by the #128 insert-scope pin — a status for rows that must not exist would silently reopen that decision); "mirror = single source of status truth" **inverted for safety gates** (a mirror scalar is the derived-state class run 65 corrupted — L2 gates re-read the ledger). New-status rule pinned: allow-list + departure gate + eligibility base updated together, never separately. (2) Migration robustness: principle pinned as a **hard migration policy** (offline-safe DDL only, no network I/O, no AutoPlant, backfills = idempotent dry-run-gated scripts per the #128 pattern); the "infer enum from DB distinct values" mechanism **rejected** — data-derived DDL makes schemas environment-dependent, the exact skew L4 refuses (also: no "migration 023" exists; with TEXT there is no enum migration at all). (3) Canary: **accepted, upgraded to L5** with two corrections — alert on **both directions** (run 65's signature was a *rise*, departed re-entering eligibility ~15,799→~21k; the run-64 stub artifact was the drop→0) and baseline stored as a **`device_state_recomputes` ledger** (counts + build stamp per recompute), which also closes a real L3 hole: recompute — the write class that caused the incident — was the only pipeline stage with no run ledger. Warn-only, threshold setting (default 5%), health-page surfacing; folded into Slice 2. | (this commit) |

| 2026-07-22 | **Backend + web-app readiness for the SE mobile client — READ-ONLY assessment (no code, no issues, no design).** Answered "what must the server side become before a 1,000-device SE app can connect": traced all 52 controllers (15 expose `SERVICE_ENGINEER`), the auth/token internals, every SE mutation's retry-safety, and measured payloads + DB limits live. **Three hard blockers, none of which mobile-side engineering can work around.** (1) **An SE cannot read a ticket** — `GET /tickets/:id|/tickets|/:id/forms` are all ZM/CSM/OH-only (`tickets.controller.ts:38,70,83`), and `GET /schedules/me` returns bare ticket IDs (`day-plan-query.service.ts:59-66`), so the M3 Ticket Detail screen has no data source (install tickets *are* SE-readable, `install.controller.ts:216` — a straight asymmetry); SEs also can't list their own vouchers or leave requests. (2) **An SE cannot log in** — `InMemoryUserStore` is a hardcoded 8-user array with exactly ONE SE (`user-store.ts:25-65`); beyond #91's durability story, `validateCredentials` uses **`scryptSync`** (`:78`) so every login blocks the event loop (~10–20 logins/sec process-wide, stalling all other traffic), the refresh Map never evicts consumed records (`refresh-token-store.ts:20,35` — unbounded growth), there is **no per-user revocation** (device loss unhandleable) and **no device identity anywhere** (zero `device_token`/`fcm`/`apns` columns in schema ⇒ push unaddressable even after #76). (3) **The process cannot hold the connections** — `PrismaPg` is constructed with only a connection string (`prisma.service.ts:28-37`), so the pool is node-postgres' default **max 10 with no acquisition timeout**, against live `statement_timeout=0` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout=0` (#106 quantified); 1,000 pollers queue unboundedly with no error. Also: **`GET /me/shared-pool` is unbounded** (`shared-pool.service.ts:31-38`) — measured worst case **1,030 tickets / ~155 KB for one SE, per poll**; only **2 of ~14** SE mutations carry an idempotency key (troubleshoot + vouchers), and the 409/Shadow-Use path never persists `clientSubmissionId` (`troubleshoot-submission.service.ts:265-303`) so **every mobile retry re-decrements van stock** on top of #101's read-then-write lost update (`:315-320`); **`ZoneScopeGuard` is a no-op for SEs** (`zone-scope.guard.ts:27-29`) and `POST /tickets/:id/troubleshoot` checks no assignment/coverage (`:112-116`), so any SE can submit against any of the 9,280 open tickets fleet-wide; **offline is currently incompatible with verification** — writes are server-stamped on arrival (`:140`) while Phase 1 searches pings `gt: submittedAt` (`verification.service.ts:159`), so a late upload excludes the very pings proving the fix; delivery is **polling-only** (no WS/SSE/seam, #76 gateway has no adapter); correlation IDs exist **only in the exception filter** (`all-exceptions.filter.ts:37`) so successful requests are untraceable. **Positive findings:** intraday accept is the reference implementation (ownership + true retry-idempotency + atomic claim, `intraday-insertion.service.ts:139-160`); recovery/VU ownership correctly enforced; **zero SE-facing advisory-lock contention** (all `try_` variants, none on request paths). Deliverable `docs/status/backend-mobile-readiness-2026-07-22.md` — sections 1–10 incl. a ranked top-10 and 8 HITL decisions (push provider, offline strategy, token lifetime, one-device-vs-many, capture-time authority, day-plan acceptance, shared-pool write scope, multi-instance target). Assessment only; nothing filed. | this commit |
| 2026-07-22 | **Feasibility: inactive devices ranked by customer business criticality — READ-ONLY analysis (no code, no issue, no design).** Can we rank broken devices by whether the customer is actively dispatching that truck (`ap_widgets.tb_vehiclemaster.TRIP_CREATION_DATETIME`)? **YES, and the data is already in FSM** — `device_states.trip_creation_datetime` has been maintained on the 30-min telemetry tick since 2026-07-17, and the one design risk that could have killed it is absent: `AutoPlantSourceReader` pages on the **immutable `device_id` with no GPS-recency predicate and no cross-run cursor** (`autoplant-source-reader.ts:8-25,84-90`), and `upsertDeviceStates` is called with **every** chunk row independent of the `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` journal skip (`snapshot-ingestion.service.ts:77`) — so **inactive devices are still re-read and still refreshed every run**. Verified live against production (bounded `SELECT`+`LIMIT≤90`): 4/4 devices match FSM **to the second** rendered `AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, independently re-confirming SYSTEM-STATE §3b's two-different-offsets design (`latest_gps_datetime` DATETIME/IST +330 vs `TRIP_CREATION_DATETIME` TIMESTAMP/already-UTC offset 0). *Methodology warning recorded in the doc:* a naive comparison shows a clean +5:30 that is a **probe artifact** (mysql2 without `dateStrings` reinterpreting in node's IST locale, plus an ad-hoc PrismaClient omitting `prisma.service.ts:36`'s `-c timezone=UTC` so Postgres defaulted to `Asia/Calcutta`) — anyone re-running must pin both sessions or they will "find" a 5.5h bug that isn't there. **Quantified (full 2,729-device population, not a sample):** HIGH (trip ≤24h) **111 (4.1%), 17 with no SE assigned**, avg **96 days** inactive · MEDIUM 984 · LOW 1,287 · NO_TRIP_DATA 347; all have open tickets, so the gap is purely **ordering**. **Headline justifying the feature: business criticality is orthogonal to the SLA bucket the engine sorts by — 84 of 111 HIGH devices sit in `LONG_PENDING`, which the canonical sort ranks LAST**, while 658 devices with no trip in >7d ARE formally assigned; tier can't be proxying for it either (top-8 HIGH companies are all SILVER). Inactive vehicles are dispatched ~4× less than fleet average (4.7%/day vs 18.4%), so the signal discriminates. **Shape:** zero schema change (`device_states` already denormalises vehicle/plant/company/transporter/sla_bucket/inactivity_hours), **cached not live** (live would need ~31 paged VPN round-trips per unfiltered view under the <100-row cap; cached costs **zero** extra AutoPlant queries), dedicated ZM-scoped page, sort HIGH → unassigned → longest-inactive. **Recommender scoring seam: LATER, not now** — evidence is strong but gated on (a) unconfirmed semantics, (b) **#124** (a new weight wouldn't appear in `config_snapshot`, un-retrofittable), (c) the `f0f3dcb` per-zone-knobs governance decision; earn it by shipping the view and checking whether ZM manual picks correlate. **Caveats reported honestly:** `active_trip_id` dates disagree with the stamp on several prod rows ⇒ the column reads more like "active-trip record last written" than "trip created" (label operationally, confirm with AutoPlant owners); 86% of stamps fall in 2026-07 so LOW means "no dispatch in a fortnight", not "seasonal"; `GREATEST` monotonicity (`snapshot-ingestion.service.ts:122`) means a downward source correction can never propagate; and freshness currently depends on a human since `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED="false"`. Deliverable `docs/analysis/inactive-devices-by-business-priority-2026-07-22.md` (verdict + evidence + 6 HITL decisions). Also noted: real trip **history** is sourceable later from `ap_widgets.tb_tripmaster` (`trip_created_date`/`trip_start_date`/`trip_status`) if last-value proves too coarse. Probe scripts deleted; no writes to AutoPlant or FSM. | this commit |
| 2026-07-22 | **Feasibility: inactive-device prioritization + last-known-location — READ-ONLY analysis (no code, no issue, no design).** Extends the same-day business-criticality analysis with the geolocation half. **YES to both; the location half is the cheapest of the two because FSM already ingests the coordinates and nobody noticed** — `AutoPlantSourceReader` has always selected `latitude, longitude` into `raw_device_snapshots.lat/lon` (`mapping.ts:143-144`): **2,769 of 2,771 inactive devices (99.93%) already have last-ping coordinates in FSM, and the stored ping timestamp matches `device_states.latest_gps_datetime` for 2,769 of 2,769**. Confirmed AutoPlant columns `latitude`/`longitude` (double), `current_location` (longtext reverse-geocoded address w/ offset), `vehicle_location_status` (SCHEDULED/ATPLANT/TOWARDSDESTINATION/EMPTY). **Corrected a premise in the brief:** AutoPlant's lat/lng does NOT update independently of pings — measured FSM last-ping vs AP live position over 30 inactive devices, **29/30 identical to the metre (0 m drift)**; the one 3,066 m exception had an AP GPS timestamp ~26 h NEWER, i.e. the truck resumed reporting (second case confirmed at ~29 h/85 m). So read-through would not drift for a *silent* device. **Snapshotting is still right, for two better reasons:** (1) **recovery** — 2 of 30 sampled devices resumed within a day, and read-through would silently swap "where we lost it" for "where it is now" exactly while an SE is en route; (2) **retention** — `raw_device_snapshots` holds history only by accident: `PARTITION_MAINTENANCE_ENABLED="false"`, so **591,033 rows are piled in `raw_device_snapshots_default`** and the newest daily partition is `y2026m07d10` (today 07-22) — the exact activation-checklist failure mode; fixing it (mandated paired with the ingestion scheduler) starts dropping daily partitions at `telemetry_retention_days=7` and **deletes the last-known location of everything inactive >7d**, while HIGH-bucket devices average ~182 days dark. **Recommended shape:** extend `device_states` (not a side table — precedent is `trip_creation_datetime`, added there 07-17; it already denormalises vehicle/plant/company/transporter/sla_bucket) with `last_known_lat/lng/address/location_at` + `vehicle_location_status`; **write-on-ping, not write-on-inactivity-transition** — same value ("location at last ping" IS "location when it went silent"), no transition detection, no flapping edge cases, correct on recovery, and it reuses the existing `unnest` upsert (`snapshot-ingestion.service.ts:114-123`, called for every chunk row per `:77`) — but **NOT** `GREATEST` for coordinates (last-observed-value, guarded on the ping advancing). Only the address + trip-state need a sync change (2 columns on an existing SELECT, zero extra AutoPlant queries); `trip_creation_datetime` already rides the 30-min tick and should **not** be moved to master sync as the brief proposed. **30-device sample (10/bucket):** 30/30 resolved in AP with a usable address; HIGH devices avg **4,355 h ≈ 182 days** dark and 9/10 `LONG_PENDING` (reproducing the orthogonality headline on an independent sample); **all 10 HIGH carried `vehicle_location_status='SCHEDULED'`**, reinforcing the "active-trip record last written" semantics; **three co-located device pairs in 30 rows** (Pune / Gurgaon / Banda — metres apart, one SE visit clears two tickets), which `plant_id` clustering cannot see. **Seams:** business-priority→scoring stays LATER (unchanged gates: semantics, #124, `f0f3dcb` governance); **location→distance scoring is a shorter path than a new weight** — the `distance` component already exists and is weighted 0.1 in `priority_rule_config` v1 but sits inert for want of input (`recommender.service.ts:371`), and device coordinates supply half of it (SE-side position still missing); coordinates are also the prerequisite for ever making `orderPlantStops` (`batch-assignment.service.ts:236-238`) route-aware. Also recommend surfacing last-known location on the existing Device Detail page (additive, no new endpoint) labelled "last known" with its timestamp. **Caveats:** address landmark offsets range 28 m–2.77 km (context, not navigation — lat/lng is truth); `vehicle_location_status` semantics sampled, not documented; a lat/lon backfill from `raw_device_snapshots` is possible **only until partition maintenance is fixed**. Deliverable `docs/analysis/inactive-device-priority-and-location-2026-07-22.md` (verdict per sub-feature + evidence + 7 HITL decisions). *Prod-query caution recorded:* an unbounded `GROUP BY` on `tb_vehiclemaster` times out — indexed `device_id` lookups only. Probe scripts deleted; no writes to AutoPlant or FSM. | this commit |

Slice 3 lands the **pair that must never be split**: `deferTicket` flips the ticket to `UNASSIGNED` (so it CAN return) **and** stamps the new ticket-level `deferred_until`; every reader of unassigned work gains `deferred_until IS NULL OR deferred_until <= <run day>`. Flip-without-date = re-dispatched the same minute (worse than the bug); date-without-flip = still stranded. **Six** readers select unassigned work and every one would have handed the ticket straight back, so the predicate is **one exported helper** (`src/ticketing/deferral.ts`) rather than six hand-written ORs — recommender TROUBLESHOOT **and** INSTALL backlog, SE Shared Pool, intraday CRITICAL insertion, Platinum cross-zone auto-escalation, ZM bulk plant-assign. **#153 is the cautionary tale directly upstream** of that call. Two cleanups fell out: `utcDayStart` was already duplicated in 2 services and slice 3 needed a third → consolidated to `src/common/utc-day.ts` (a gate disagreeing with the dispatch day by an hour releases deferred work early); and `getSharedPool` had **no clock** — it read `new Date()` directly, which the RED caught (spec ran in June, wall clock in July) → now takes an injectable `now` like its sibling sweeps. Migration `20260722120000_ticket_deferred_until` is additive/nullable, **deliberately un-indexed** (unselective predicate; a partial index would be raw SQL = fresh drift). **Drift gate: no `tickets`/`deferred_until` drift** — only `runtime_lock`, the documented booted-DB artifact; **not re-baselined**. The two tests that pinned `FORMALLY_ASSIGNED` are **inverted here on purpose** — exactly what they were written to gate — and now assert `UNASSIGNED` **and** the date. **Full suite: 291 files / 3 skipped (294); 1196 passed / 5 skipped (1201); exit 0; 686 s** (reconciles: 288+3 files, 1182+14 tests). **B1 is functionally complete. #146 still OPEN: slice 4 (B2 soft bias — untouched) and slice 5 (admin parity, in-scope, not deferrable).** | `0b77bdd` + docs |

| 2026-07-29 | **[#176](./issues/176-kpi-transparency-operational-population.md) DONE — dashboard KPI transparency: one operational population, named and reconciled (operator ask, 10 deliverables).** Investigation first: the dashboard showed Total Devices 50,270 / Active Fleet 17,415 / Inactive 3,476 while Zone and Company Overview divided by **23,238**. Root cause — `zoneOverview`'s totals query had **no `is_departed` predicate**, while its numerator excluded departed devices *structurally* (`DeviceStateService.recompute` sets `is_inactive = NOT departed AND …`; verified live: 0 rows are both departed and inactive). So `inactive / total` divided an operational numerator by an operational+warehouse denominator; `companyPlantOverview` and `fleetDirectory` carried the same omission, and `fleetDirectory`'s own docstring claimed a reconciliation with the Active Fleet KPI (23,238 vs 17,415) **it never had**. Because warehouse stock is uneven (West 14.7% departed vs South 39.4%) the distortion **re-ordered the Zone Performance Scorecard** — South read 21.4% inactive against an actual 35.2% (1.65x). A second, latent defect surfaced during the fix: zone/company rows were built from the *inactive* query, so a zone or plant with a fleet but zero inactive devices **vanished**, taking its operational devices out of the column totals. **Backend:** one shared `FLEET_COUNT_COLUMNS` aggregate (five `COUNT(*) FILTER` columns) selected by every level — fleet / zone / company x plant / company / plant — differing only in `GROUP BY`, which makes the drift class *unrepresentable* rather than merely fixed; rows now driven by the counts query (company x plant 135 -> 205 live); `fleetSummary` gains the breakdown + `catalogDevices` (renamed from `sourceDevices`) + `lastMasterSyncAt`/`lastSnapshotAt`; `fleetDirectory` gains the breakdown + per-entity `lastSnapshotAt`/`lastActivityAt`; new **`GET /api/dashboard/fleet-composition`** funnel with every drop named. **Admin:** `lib/kpiCatalog.ts` (one registry: name, family source/operational/derived, definition, counts, excludes, source table, refresh, formula, reconciliation identity) driving both the new zero-dependency **`KpiInfo`** tooltip on every KPI card and counted column header **and** `docs/kpi-definitions.md`; renames **Total Devices -> AutoPlant Catalog** (sync stamp on the card), **Active Fleet -> Operational Fleet**, **Inactive / Total -> Inactive Operational**; two new sections on all three manager dashboards (**Operational Fleet** 6-KPI strip + **Fleet Composition** funnel); Operational/Warehouse/Healthy/Inactive %/Fleet Health % columns across Zone Overview, Scorecard, Company/Plant Overview and Fleet Directory (+ Mirrored/Last Snapshot/Last Activity in the Directory), exports updated. **Decisions:** D-1 (HITL) per-entity *AutoPlant Devices* is **not buildable** — the catalog is one global counter and non-mirrored source rows are never persisted with a plant, so the Directory ships **Mirrored Devices** (operational+warehouse), which reconciles exactly; recorded as a known limitation, not a deferral. D-2 **business logic untouched** — *inactive* keeps its exact predicate, thresholds/SLA bands/departure semantics/deactivated-plant exclusion all unchanged; only denominators, row construction, naming and presentation moved. D-3 `healthyOperational` is **counted, not subtracted** (complement predicate, same set) so `healthy + inactive = operational` holds structurally. D-4 a ZM's funnel **omits** the catalog steps rather than showing a pan-India figure. **Live verification (dev DB, master sync run 90):** all 14 identity checks pass — catalog 50,270 -> mirrored 24,225 (-26,045 never mirrored) -> on live plants 23,238 (-987 deactivated) -> operational **17,415** + warehouse **5,823**; operational splits **13,939** healthy + **3,476** inactive. Every figure in the operator's spec hit exactly. New `test/dashboard-kpi-reconciliation.e2e-spec.ts` (14 tests) asserts the identities **over the whole database**, not a fixture — it would have failed on the pre-fix code with the production dataset loaded; new `test/kpi-transparency.test.tsx` (14 tests); shared `test/fixtures/fleet.ts` builders that cannot encode an inconsistent numerator/denominator. Backend dashboard e2e **24/24**; admin **367 tests / 87 files green**; tsc clean both apps. Full reference: **`docs/kpi-definitions.md`**. | this commit |

| 2026-08-02/03 | **[#184](./issues/184-vitest-worker-exited-unexpectedly.md) DONE — all 8 ACs, the suite-repair block fully closed.** AC-1: instrumented via a workspace-scoped `pnpm patch` on `tinypool@1.1.1` (`patches/tinypool@1.1.1.patch`, tracked in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`) logging the parent-observed child `(exit code, signal)`, plus new `test/crash-diagnostics.ts` (in-child `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection`/`exit` logger) — both no-ops unless `TINYPOOL_CRASH_LOG` is set. Deliberately did **not** edit `node_modules/.pnpm` directly (content-addressed, hardlink-shared across every project on the machine); `pnpm patch`/`patch-commit` is the safe equivalent. AC-2: measured rates — `settings-write` window ~1/24, `plant-zone-change-impact` window 0/20, full suite (317 files, default per-file-fork mode) **2 of 3 runs crashed**, independently reproducing the AMENDED note's elevated rate in a second session. AC-1 evidence across 6 captured crashes: **5/6 = NTSTATUS `0xC0000409` (`STATUS_STACK_BUFFER_OVERRUN`)**, mid-file, zero preceding uncaught-exception/rejection log lines; **1/6 = `0xC0000142` (`STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED`)** on a worker that died before any setupFile ran. Zero Windows Event Viewer entries (Application + System) at either crash timestamp — no WER trail. AC-3: root cause is a Windows-native per-forked-child fault, **not file-content-driven** (one crash hit `global-guard-validation.e2e-spec.ts`, which doesn't even boot `AppModule`) and **not reduced by fewer forks** — `poolOptions.forks.singleFork` was tested empirically (collapses ~315 forks to ~1) and still crashed 2/3 full-suite runs at the identical signature, while making the blast radius catastrophically worse (losing the entire rest of the run — 60 to 195 files — instead of 1-2). H2/H4/H5 ruled out with direct evidence; H1 narrowed to "native-layer fault, not classic V8 heap OOM" with `@swc/core-win32-x64-msvc`'s native transform binary as the leading (not conclusively pinned) suspect — the only native addon confirmed freshly loaded per forked child (`pg-native` isn't installed). AC-4: not fixable in `src/`/`test/` logic, so shipped **auto-recovery** instead — `scripts/run-tests.mjs` now diffs vitest's own per-file completion lines against the full expected glob to find exactly which file(s) a crash dropped, and retries only those (budget 3), fixing a real bug caught in testing (passing all ~317 file paths as explicit CLI args hit Windows' command-line length limit and looked exactly like "every file crashed" — the full-suite entry point now lets vitest's own `include` glob resolve the set instead). Validated against the same 3-full-suite-run count as AC-2: run 1 crashed (1 file dropped), run 2 crashed (2 files dropped), both fully recovered via 1 retry each, **317/317 reconciled both times**; run 3 clean throughout. All 3 final results complete and correct — 0 unreconciled files, 0 genuine test failures — even though the underlying native crash still fired in 2/3 attempts; the crash itself was not eliminated, its silent damage was. `singleFork` left available (`VITEST_SINGLE_FORK` env-gated, off by default) with a comment recording why it was rejected. AC-7/AC-8: `settings-write` and `plant-zone-change-impact` both cleared as **non-causal** — neither crashed in its dedicated 20-loop window nor was either among the two full-suite crashes' dropped files (which were three different, unrelated files each time); #180 R1.2's `cleanupLeftoverPlant()` fix is confirmed to have fully resolved `plant-zone-change-impact`'s prior instability (H3). No `src/` change. **Suite-repair investigation (#180–185) now fully closed.** | this commit |

| 2026-08-04 | **[#204](./issues/204-time-semantics-day-boundary-implementation.md) partially landed — the IST operating day + dispatch cron timezone; leave windows and the admin planner remain.** First implementation slice off the [#197](./issues/197-mobile-pilot-readiness-remediation-epic.md) epic, executing the [#198](./issues/198-decision-day-boundary-and-dispatch-clock.md) ruling now recorded as **CONTEXT.md Decisions §19**. **The design correction is the story:** `utcDayStart` was used against *two different column types*, so swapping its body — the obvious implementation — would have silently shifted one family of queries by 5h30m. Postgres `DATE` carries no timezone and Prisma marshals it at UTC midnight, so `@db.Date` comparisons (`deferred_until`, `deferred_to_date`, `date_from`, `date_to`) need **UTC midnight of the IST calendar date**, while `@db.Timestamptz` (`removed_at`) needs **the real instant IST midnight occurred**. New `src/common/ist-day.ts` exports both (`istDate` / `istDayStartInstant`) with the distinction documented; 12 of 13 call sites are the DATE form, and the one timestamptz site (`me-tickets-query.service.ts`, the "removed from plan today" window) was therefore *already* subtly wrong under the old helper. All 13 sites migrated across 12 services, `utc-day.ts` deleted so one definition remains. Cron pinned via `@Cron(..., { timeZone: BUSINESS_TIMEZONE })` — asserted **behaviourally** (boot `ScheduleModule`, read the registered job, next fire must be 23:30 UTC = 05:00 IST) rather than by decorator metadata; it failed `expected 5 to be 23` first, confirming it had been firing at 05:00 in the *host's* zone. TDD red→green on both. **Found and deliberately NOT changed — needs a ruling:** `business-sweep-scheduler.service.ts:89` has its *own* `previousUtcDayStart` (different symbol, so the sweep missed it) feeding the system-efficiency cube, so analytics still buckets UTC while operations now bucket IST — a ticket closed at 02:00 IST sits on today's Day Plan but yesterday's cube. Not a like-for-like fix: the cube already holds UTC-computed rows, so changing it rewrites history or leaves a seam, and the other four cubes are unaudited. Three options written up on #204. **Handoff for the remainder: [`204-HANDOFF.md`](./issues/204-HANDOFF.md)** (leave-window IST interpretation — design settled, `windowEnd` is exclusive so an IST day maps to `[IST midnight, next IST midnight)`, and admin's `datetime-local` instants must not be re-interpreted; admin planner alignment; then #213, which must not rebuild the four dispatch pieces that already exist). | this commit |

Corrected in place instead of duplicated: #186 (scope completion), #29 (**AC#6 was factually false** — admin never bound the intraday insertion columns; zero `intraday-insertions` refs in apps/admin; FE-13 deferred it and filed no follow-up), #38 (photo-lightbox AC silently broken by #81's photoRef→media-id change), #25 (admin setter missing AVAILABLE though the backend permits managers to set it), #66 (records that #161 later shipped the server signal its Option B anticipated), #85 ("admin has its own notification surfaces" was an assumption; admin consumes zero notifications). **Three anticipated decisions turned out already decided** and were filed as work, not questions: `/api` alias retirement (ratified as time-boxed → #212), SLA labels (PRD:824-834 specifies severity names — admin is the deviating surface, mobile is conformant → #208), manager notifications (PRD specifies Action Required + badge, explicitly *not* an inbox page → #206). Headlines, all source-cited or live-probed: mobile never refreshes tokens after mount (15-min access TTL → every screen flips to a false "Offline" after 15 minutes; admin has both proactive + 401-interceptor refresh, mobile has neither); guard-level 401/403 bodies carry no `code` so the recovery/install/intraday/availability write paths throw `Error(undefined)` (live-probed); admin voucher review renders the raw `media_objects` id as `<img src>` — every mobile-uploaded receipt is a broken image (no admin media client exists; `GET /media/:id` unconsumed); admin login revokes the mobile session unconditionally (revoke-all-by-userId, no device comparison); mobile ignores the server's `removedFromPlanAt`/`deferredToDate` contract (re-derives via in-memory diff, cold-start removals invisible, DEFER≡REMOVE collapsed); admin consumes zero notifications (15 produced types, 1 consumed anywhere — manager-action states invisible); admin cannot set AVAILABLE though the backend's manager legs permit it; admin ticket filter missing 9/17 statuses; UNZONED's two disjoint definitions confirmed from source (#192); dispatch cron TZ unpinned (05:00 UTC = 10:30 IST on a UTC host); `BigInt(id)` 500 on intraday routes (live-reproduced); SE_ACCEPTANCE body still leaks a ticket UUID (`intraday-insertion.service.ts:214`, #76's own flagged defect, unfixed). **#58 corrected in place** (no new issue — dup-check found it owns the troubleshoot-photo AC): its "blocked on #81 (unbuilt)" reason is stale, #81 is done, pattern established by Voucher/Install forms; also corrected its stale "409 inline" claim (ConflictScreen is wired). `device_tokens` migration re-confirmed unapplied on dev DB (runbook, not an issue — step 1 of the readiness doc's path). #194 (concurrent session) noted as owner of the dev-login seed gap. | this commit |
| 2026-08-04 | **[#204](./issues/204-time-semantics-day-boundary-implementation.md) finished — leave/availability windows and the admin planner now read the IST day; all six ACs met.** Picked up `204-HANDOFF.md` (now consumed → `docs/archive/`) and built the two items it left. **B8 (leave windows):** `istWindowStart`/`istWindowEnd` join the two day helpers in `src/common/ist-day.ts`. A bare `YYYY-MM-DD` names an **IST calendar day**, so it resolves to `[IST midnight of that date, IST midnight of the *next* date)` — the `+1 day` on the end is not a flourish, it is what makes the named day covered, because the active-window predicate is `windowStart <= now AND windowEnd > now` (`se-availability.service.ts:45,73`), i.e. **end-exclusive**. Wired into both write points that take a free-text window — `leave-request.controller.ts` (mobile sends date-only, `LeaveRequestFormScreen.tsx:46`) and `engineers.controller.ts :seId/availability` (mobile's field is free text, so date-only is reachable there too). Anything that is not a bare date passes to `new Date` untouched: admin's `datetime-local` is already `toISOString()`d (`SeManagementPage.tsx:89`) and is a real instant, not an IST calendar day. Two consequential behaviour changes, both falling out of end-exclusivity rather than chosen: `WINDOW_ORDER` is now `end <= start` (date-only made a zero-length window newly reachable — an end date exactly one day before the start resolves to *exactly* the start, and a leave request granting zero unavailability is a silent no-op, not a success), and a non-existent date (`2026-02-31`) stays Invalid Date instead of letting `Date.UTC` roll it over, preserving the `INVALID_WINDOW` 400. **Admin planner:** `istIsoDate`/`addIsoDays` added to `src/lib/datetime.ts` (shared home, mirroring the backend's one-definition posture); `PlannerPage.tsx` derives its 7-day window from them instead of device-local `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate` — `planned_date` is a `@db.Date` bucketed on the IST day, so a ZM planning at 02:00 IST was writing intents onto the previous day's column unless their machine was set to IST. **B7 went live and was fixed in the same slice — the issue predicted this exact thing** ("out of scope *unless the ruling makes one live*"). Once a window is stored as the instants bounding its IST days, `iso.slice(0, 10)` renders a start **one day early**: `LeaveRequestsPage.tsx:48`, `SeManagementPage.tsx:286-287` and mobile `leaveDisplay.ts:28-30` all did that. Replaced with a named start/end pair on both clients (`istWindowStartDate`/`istWindowEndDate`; `formatLeaveWindowStart`/`formatLeaveWindowEnd`) — the **end** helper steps back a millisecond before taking the IST date, because the stored end is exclusive and the raw instant names the day *after* the last covered one. Duplicated per app rather than lifted into `@fsm/shared` on purpose: that package ships built `dist/`, so a runtime helper there couples every consumer's test run to a rebuild step. TDD red→green throughout: the leave e2e failed `expected 'AVAILABLE' to be 'ON_LEAVE'` at 00:15 IST first, which is the B8 bug stated exactly. The planner test **forces the device zone to UTC** for its render cases — on an IST workstation the old code gives the right answer by accident and the test would have proven nothing. Verification: full backend suite + `tsc --noEmit`; admin 92 files / 414 tests + `tsc --noEmit`; mobile 46 suites / 313 tests + `tsc --noEmit`. **Pre-existing failure found, NOT caused by this work and not fixed here:** `voucher-controller.e2e-spec.ts` fails 2 tests in a full run and 3 in isolation — it has no fixture of its own for the seeded `se.north@fsm.test` and depends on whichever earlier spec last created that `engineer_master` row (and with which zone). Proven pre-existing by re-running it with this slice's backend files reverted to `5afc920`: byte-identical failure. **The analytics-day open question was put to the operator and RULED: move analytics to the IST day and recompute**, accepting rewritten history for the affected range — landed as a CONTEXT.md §19 addendum and filed as [#214](./issues/214-analytics-day-ist-recompute.md), which requires a written finding on each of the four unaudited cubes before any recompute runs. **#204 now has nothing open on it.** **Still open, unchanged and deliberately not decided here:** the analytics day is still UTC (`business-sweep-scheduler.service.ts:89`'s own `previousUtcDayStart`) while operations are now IST — three options written up on #204, needs an operator ruling, and the other four cubes remain unaudited. | `c84cb2b` + this commit |
| 2026-08-04 | **[#213](./issues/213-configurable-dispatch-schedule.md) DONE — the dispatch schedule becomes operator-owned configuration, and the overlap guard becomes real.** Four slices, each red→green. **(1) Schedule.** `system_settings.dispatch_cron` is the source of truth; `BUSINESS_SWEEP_DISPATCH_CRON` seeds the row once (create-only, so a redeploy never clobbers an operator's setting) and is never read again. A write validates → persists → re-points the **live** job, proven by the registered job's next fire moving 23:30→00:30 UTC *inside one process*. **Notable:** `cron` was NOT added as a dependency — `@nestjs/schedule` v4 doesn't re-export it and it isn't resolvable from `apps/backend`, so `dispatch-cron.ts` takes the `CronTime` constructor off a live job. That began as lockfile avoidance (the lock carries another session's uncommitted state) but is the better answer anyway: **the parser that validates an operator's expression is byte-for-byte the parser that will run it** — a separately-versioned copy could accept an expression the scheduler then rejects, which is precisely the accepted-then-silently-dead schedule AC-4 exists to prevent. The `@Cron` decorator survives, but only to *register* the job under a stable name/timezone: it evaluates once at class-decoration time, long before a DB is reachable, so `DispatchScheduleService.onApplicationBootstrap` re-points it (bootstrap, not module-init — `@nestjs/schedule` mounts decorator jobs in its own bootstrap hook). **A hole closed alongside:** `PUT /api/settings/:key` is OH-only but writes *any* key — left open it bypasses both guarantees at once (unparseable value stored AND the job never re-registered, so the schedule reads as changed while dispatch fires at the old hour). `SettingsService.set` now refuses keys with a specialised writer and names the endpoint that owns them. **(2) Guard.** The scheduler's private `inFlight` field is **deleted**; the guard lives inside `runForActiveZones`, the one path the cron tick and the manual trigger share, so neither can bypass it — the exact defect the issue was filed for. `runForActiveZones` returns `RAN | CONFLICT` (a union, not an exception: a refusal is a first-class answer, and the union forces every future caller to acknowledge it); six existing call sites narrowed via a shared `expectRan` helper rather than loosened. The operator-requested regression case passes — scheduled run in flight + manual trigger → exactly one run *and* a populated 409 naming the zone, the start time in IST and the actor. **Stated honestly on the issue:** the guard is per-zone and **in-process**; it is what gives an operator a clear answer, not a distributed lock — the cross-process guarantee remains the advisory locks + idempotency (#100). **(3) Ledger reason** — migration `20260804150000_dispatch_run_reason`, blank stored as null not `''`, surfaced in run detail. **(4) Admin** — Settings › Dispatch Schedule tab (shows timezone + next fire, so an operator can confirm the change *took*), and the Run-dispatch button disables itself with the reason while a run is in flight, polled because the run it guards against is usually the cron starting unprompted. **Deliberately not done:** other sweep crons stay env-configured (did not fall out for free — each needs its own key, seed, validation route and re-registration hook); no range restriction on the expression (`* * * * *` is valid and accepted — narrowing what is *sensible* is a product question #213 doesn't rule on). **Dev DB needs `pnpm prisma migrate deploy`** for the new column. Verification: admin 93 files / 420 tests + `tsc` clean; backend `tsc` clean; full backend suite green apart from the pre-existing `voucher-controller` failure documented on the #204 line above. | `5bf1c02` + this commit |
