### Funnel status (verified against the tree 2026-07-09)

The end-to-end funnel is **code-complete and wired**; activation is blocked by data + two paused ops
switches, **not** by missing code. "Code" = mechanism present & tested; "Data" = the dev/prod DB lacks
the rows the stage consumes; "Paused" = an audited ops toggle deliberately left OFF.

| Stage | Owner issue | Code status | What blocks activation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ingest (masters → snapshots → `device_states`) | #96, #97 | ✅ done & committed (`97-PROGRESS.md:299`) | **Paused** — `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=true` after zone ratification (B8); live-VPN verify = HITL (external access). Data present (`device_states` 18,528) |
| Ticket creation (inactive+eligible → OPEN ticket) | #112 | ✅ done (`integration-sync.service.ts:78→80`, `:130→132`) | **Paused (business)** — `eligibility_mode='pgi'` over empty `pgi_history` (B7) → 0 eligible → 0 ticket-candidates today. Interim `all-deployed` proxy exists but is an ops/business decision |
| Recommender (score + hard filters → SUGGESTED) | #10, #72, #75, **Phase 4 SE Mgmt** | ✅ done (`recommender.service.ts`; SE data now admin-enterable) | **Data (self-serve)** — SEs are entered in-app via `/engineers/manage` (`EngineerAdminService`, OH/CSM cross-zone, ZM home-zone-clamped, SE denied; in-zone coverage only). Empty `engineer_master`/`se_coverage` still → UNASSIGNABLE, but the decision "where do SEs come from" is resolved: admin-entered, no feed/seed |
| Dispatch (SUGGESTED → Day Plan, transactional/idempotent) | #100, #113 | ✅ done (`batch-assignment.service.ts:52`, `dispatch-run.service.ts`, cron `dispatch-scheduler.service.ts:50`) | **Paused** — shares `BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED` (default OFF). Also **Data** — 0 candidates upstream |
| Field loop (intraday / verification / install sweeps) | #29/#30, #18/#19, #34, #108 | ✅ done — 10 sweeps registered (`business-sweep-scheduler.service.ts:143-191`) | **Paused** — `BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED` (default OFF). **Code hardening:** #102 install zone scope ✅ (`f48108f`); #101 guarded transitions **partial** (intraday accept-vs-timeout leg ✅ `9940534`, rest open); #103 hot-FK indexes open |

**Root blockers, ranked:** (1) engineers/coverage data — a *tested* seed script would unblock the pending
decision without taking it; (2) B7 eligibility decision; (3) the two ops toggles. All code seams exist.

### Activation checklist (env/ops enable order — do NOT flip casually)

The schedulers are OFF by default. When activating for real (a deliberate ops step, post zone
ratification), enable in this order and mind the couplings:

1. **`eligibility_mode`** (audited `system_settings`, OH-owned) — `pgi` (canonical) requires a live SAP
   PGI feed; until then the interim `all-deployed` proxy makes current-fitment-on-ACTIVE devices eligible.
   Harmless to set while schedulers are OFF (it only bites on the next pipeline recompute).
2. **`INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=true`** — turns the pipeline self-running (masters daily + telemetry
   every 30 min). **MUST be enabled together with `PARTITION_MAINTENANCE_ENABLED=true`** — without it,
   after the 3-day partition create-ahead runway new pings silently fall into the DEFAULT partition and
   retention never runs (handoff §4 item 5 / `docs/archive/HANDOFF-autoplant-ingestion-2026-07-07.md`). Treat the
   two as one switch. Also set `INGESTION_STALE_RUN_MIN` above the telemetry cadence (reaper safety).
3. **`BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED=true`** — turns on dispatch (`business-dispatch` cron) + the 10 field-loop
   sweeps (verification / intraday-timeout / cross-zone / install-verification / …). Needs SE + coverage
   data present (else every ticket is UNASSIGNABLE) — SEs are admin-entered via `/engineers/manage`.
   **SECOND SWITCH PAIR — sweeps depend on ingestion (#148, 2026-07-22).** The verification and
   install-activation sweeps resolve a 24-hour window against `raw_device_snapshots`, so they are only
   meaningful while step 2 is actually running. **This pair is now ENFORCED IN CODE**, not just
   documented: both sweeps refuse to expire a window until the telemetry watermark
   (`snapshot_runs.data_as_of`) has advanced past the submission / activation
   (`VerificationService.windowExpired`, `InstallLifecycleService.activationWindowExpired`). Before
   #148 a submission made while ingestion was paused aged into an **irreversible**
   `FAILED_VERIFICATION` / `FAILED_ACTIVATION` — rolling back `PRE_VERIFICATION` inventory and pushing
   a false failure to the SE — regardless of whether the SE had fixed the device.
   **Consequence to watch:** the guard converts a wrong verdict into a window that stays PENDING
   indefinitely while telemetry is frozen. That is the correct trade (it can only ever delay a failure,
   never cause one), but it must be **visible** — surfacing stalled windows on `/build-health` is
   #148 slice 3, still open. **Never** "fix" a stalled window by auto-expiring after a grace period;
   that re-creates the original bug with a longer fuse.

> **Note on the state of these flags today (2026-07-22):** the checklist above describes the *enable
> order*, not current reality. `apps/backend/.env` currently has `BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED="true"` with
> `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED="false"` and `PARTITION_MAINTENANCE_ENABLED="false"` — i.e. **step 3 is
> on while step 2 is off**, exactly the inversion the pairing above warns about. That is what made
> #148 an armed defect rather than a hypothetical one, and it is why the pairing is now enforced in
> code. Corrected in `SYSTEM-STATE` by #149.

Manual HTTP triggers (`POST /api/integration/run-pipeline`, `POST /api/schedules/dispatch-run`, the
per-sweep triggers) drive the exact same code paths **without** enabling any cron — use them for watched
runs while the flags stay OFF.

## Follow-ups (deep review)
- 278 — [The SE detail panel can show an earlier day's plan as "today's"](./issues/278-engineer-detail-current-schedule-no-date-predicate.md) → 269, 147  *(filed 2026-08-20 from #269's build, and deliberately not folded into it. `EngineersQueryService.currentDayPlan` and `currentAssignments` resolve "the SE's current schedule" as `findFirst({ seId, liveScheduleFilter() }, orderBy: dispatchedAt desc)` with **no date predicate**, so a still-`ACTIVE` plan from an earlier day is a legitimate match and, if it sorts first, is presented as today's — ticket count, schedule dates and every plant stop from the wrong day. Exactly the defect #147 fixed for the SE's own Day Plan, where the fix carries the reasoning verbatim: "the date predicate, not the ordering, decides which plan is today's". #269 closed the **capacity** half of this on the same service — `activeTicketCount` is now date-scoped through the shared `committedDayLoad` and agrees with the recommender — but this neighbouring read is not a capacity question: it is *which schedule is current*, and answering it means ruling on what a genuinely multi-day plan should show and whether "no plan today" should render as empty (the SE's own Day Plan's answer) or as stale work labelled with its own dates. A small design call rather than a drive-by, and #269 carried no AC over it. #178's closure recycle shrinks the stale population but does not remove the case; this is the defence that does not depend on a sweep having run. Pinning it needs the older schedule given the **later** `dispatchedAt`, so ordering alone cannot produce the right answer.)*
- 257 — **✅ DONE 2026-08-20 (`16dbc9c`), filed and fixed in one session.** [The stored dispatch schedule is never applied at boot](./issues/257-dispatch-schedule-never-applied-at-boot.md) -> 213  *(filed 2026-08-20 from a scheduler-engine analysis that took seriously the ERROR line opening **every** e2e app boot — hundreds of boots across three suite runs, 100% of them: `stored dispatch schedule '0 5 * * *' could not be applied … No Cron Job was found (business-dispatch)`. Root cause is framework hook ordering: `@Cron` jobs are only **mounted** into `SchedulerRegistry` by `SchedulerOrchestrator`'s own `onApplicationBootstrap`, and Nest runs bootstrap hooks deepest-module-first (`b.distance - a.distance`), which on this graph puts `DispatchScheduleService`'s hook **before** the mount — so `getCronJob` threw on every boot and the job stayed on the compile-time default. Invisible only because the seeded value equals the default: the day an operator changes the dispatch hour, the next restart silently reverts dispatch to 05:00 IST while the API reports the stored value — #213's guarantee held within a process and broke across processes. Fixed with the ordering Nest actually guarantees rather than a graph massage: `applyStoredSchedule()` called from `main.ts` after `app.listen()`; the bootstrap hook seeds the row and applies only behind `registry.doesExist`, logging at `log` when it defers. Pinned by `dispatch-schedule-boot.e2e-spec.ts`, which writes `0 6 * * *` BEFORE booting — the restart scenario the existing #213 test could not see, since its stored value equalled the default and the assertion passed whether the apply ran or not.)*
- 256 — **✅ DONE 2026-08-20 (`bd7dd06`).** [`plant-zone-change-impact` fails every night between 00:00 and 05:30 IST](./issues/256-plant-zone-change-impact-utc-ist-day-fixture.md) -> 107, 204  *(filed 2026-08-20 from #255's second verification run, and **not** caused by it. Two full suites on the same tree disagreed: the 18:03 IST run gave the known 2 failures, the 23:33 IST run gave 3. The extra one reproduces **in isolation**, so it is neither a fixture collision nor #184 — it is the clock. `zone-mapping.service.ts:209` reads the **IST** day (`istDate(new Date())`, #204's ruling, correctly applied) while the spec's fixture stamps its `work_schedules` row with `setUTCHours(0,0,0,0)`, a **UTC** day. IST is UTC+05:30, so for the 5.5 hours from 00:00 to 05:30 IST the two name different dates, `dateTo >= day` misses, and `dispatchedTodayCount` is 0 instead of 1; the other 18.5 hours it passes. Confirmed against the clock at the failing isolation run — 05:13 IST / 23:43 UTC. **This is the third distinct non-regression reason a full run can be red**, after `voucher-controller` (#187) and the #184 crash, and it is the one that matters most for **#107**: a nightly CI schedule lands squarely inside the failing window. Fix is the fixture, plus a sweep of `setUTCHours(0, 0, 0, 0)` across `test/` for the same UTC-vs-IST mismatch — #204's own session found two, this is a third. Pinning it needs care: the host runs on IST, so the naive test cannot tell the two apart for most of the day.)*
- 255 — **✅ DONE 2026-08-19 (`def334b`).** [16 e2e files share one hardcoded SE id and race on a partial unique Prisma cannot see](./issues/255-shared-se-fixture-races-partial-unique.md) -> 107  *(filed 2026-08-19 from the #247-#249 full-suite verification. The run reported 4 failed files; only the known `voucher-controller` pair was real — the other three (`soft-state-controller`, `ticket-forms-read`, `troubleshoot-controller`) threw in **`beforeAll`**, so **zero** of their tests ran, and all three pass 3/3 in isolation. Root cause is exact: sixteen e2e files hardcode `SE_ID = '22222222-…-222222222222'` (the in-memory auth seed) and each upserts a **DEDICATED** `se_coverage` row for it at its **own** plant. The upsert's conflict target is `(se_id, plant_id)` — different plants, no conflict, so both INSERT — but `20260618121101…/migration.sql:60` also declares `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX se_coverage_dedicated_se_key ON se_coverage(se_id) WHERE coverage_type = 'DEDICATED'`, a **partial** unique that is raw SQL by necessity and therefore invisible to the Prisma client. The loser is rejected. Structural, not incidental: all sixteen use DEDICATED and their own plant, so whether it fires is purely which files a worker pool runs concurrently. A second, deterministic bug rides along — `afterAll` deletes `engineer_master` before `se_coverage` and violates `se_coverage_se_id_fkey`. **Why it matters:** a suite-level `beforeAll` throw reports zero tests, so those files silently contribute no coverage on a colliding run; every red run needs hand-triage into "real" vs "collided", which is precisely the triage cost that made an earlier session wrongly indict its own change (#184 note); and **#107's unattended CI cannot hand-triage at all**. Preferred fix is the inverse of the obvious one: one DEDICATED coverage per SE is a real business invariant, so the partial index is right and sixteen files each asserting their own is the mistake — seed it once in global setup rather than per file.)*
- 254 — **✅ DONE 2026-08-20 (`16dbc9c`).** [`plant-eligibility-refresh` fires *after* the dispatch it feeds (unpinned cron, #240 family)](./issues/254-plant-eligibility-refresh-cron-unpinned.md) -> 138, 240  *(filed 2026-08-19 while writing down the daily cron chain for #247 — found by documenting it, not by a test. `plant-eligibility-refresh-scheduler.service.ts:59` registers with **no `timeZone`**, and its own docstring states the bug while stating the intent: "04:30 **UTC**, i.e. shortly before the default 05:00 dispatch tick". That dispatch is 05:00 **IST** (23:30 UTC), so on a UTC host the refresh lands at 10:00 IST — five hours **after** the batch it exists to feed, which then consumes a `plant_eligible_floating_se` MV up to a day stale. Identical shape to the `schedule-closure` defect #240 fixed; that fix was scoped to two jobs and this one was not among them, and #247's new sweep was pinned from the start — so this is the last unpinned link. **Impact is bounded, not nil**: #138 slice 1 re-checks `coverage_type`/`is_active` live and slice 2 refreshes after each master sync, so this scheduler is the *backstop* — the effect is that the backstop's staleness bound is a day wider than intended on the plant-SE geometry the floating leg still reads from the MV. Fix is three lines plus a behavioural pin; **the pin must be verified under `TZ=UTC`**, because the dev host runs on IST and removing the option does not turn such a test red locally. Carries a fourth AC to rule explicitly on the report cubes' day/month boundaries, which are still UTC while the rest of the system reads days as IST.)*
- 253 — [A submission before the return date strands the primary SLA paused forever](./issues/253-vu-pause-stranded-by-early-submission.md) -> 247  *(filed 2026-08-19 from #247's build, and deliberately not folded into it. `troubleshoot-submission.service.ts:167` resolves an OPEN vehicle report and — by an explicit decision recorded there at the time — does **not** resume the SLA, deferring reason-aware resumption to #247. #247 has now built that, and the comment points straight at what is left: if the SE submits **before** the return date (reachable via the shared pool, or via #249's own deferral override), the report flips `RESOLVED` while the cycle stays `VEHICLE_UNAVAILABLE`-paused, and `VehicleReturnResumeService` only ever looks at **OPEN** reports — so no automatic path can ever see it again and the primary clock is frozen for the life of the Failure Cycle. That is exactly the defect #247 exists to remove, reached by the one route its sweep cannot cover. Not in-scope-and-quietly-fixed because closing it means a **third** automatic writer of `sla_paused = false`, and #247 AC2's "single writer" phrasing deserves an explicit re-ruling — it was written about the *date-driven* resumer, but it is written down. Manual recovery exists (`resumeSla` works on a report id regardless of status) and nothing surfaces that it is needed. Not reachable on the ordinary path, where the sweep has already run before the SE arrives.)*
- 252 — Surface the two "engine did not decide" counters on the dispatch transparency zone card → 242, 238  *(filed 2026-08-19 from #242's build. #242's own `## UI surfaces` line claimed the new ledger counts "surface through the existing dispatch transparency page without layout change" — **false, and corrected in place**: `DispatchRunZoneCard` (`dispatch-transparency-query.service.ts:26`) projects neither `bucketless_dropped` nor #238's `withheld_below_threshold`, so neither reaches the admin page at all. The three populations route to different teams — `unassignable` = the engine looked and found nobody (Ops), `withheld_below_threshold` = it deliberately did not look yet (policy), `bucketless_dropped` = it could not look (data: an un-recomputed `device_states.sla_bucket`) — and on the dev mirror the third is **5,127 of 6,464** OPEN+UNASSIGNED Troubleshoot tickets, i.e. the majority of the pool, so `recommended + unassignable` reads as the whole funnel while most of it sits outside. Small and additive: project both on the zone card, keep the three labels distinct, and render a NULL as "not recorded" rather than `0` (the column is nullable precisely so a pre-#242 run does not claim a measurement nobody took). **No v2 reference image exists for the dispatch pages** — verified by listing the directory; the built page is the authority and is to be extended, not redrawn. #242's AC5 is a ledger requirement and is met; this is the rendering half, filed rather than smuggled into a backend slice.)*
- 218 — Deployment-lifecycle drift: detection first, then the DI fix → 128, 130, 217  *(filed 2026-08-07 from an AutoPlant↔FSM reconciliation against an operator-supplied known-good Excel, and **hand-verified by the operator before approval**. `MasterSyncService`'s lifecycle pass has never run on the Nest-wired path — scheduler and "Run Ingestion Now" — because `master-sync.service.ts:2-3` import `DeviceDepartureService` and `PlantEligibleFloatingSeService` with `import type`; the erased import makes `design:paramtypes` emit `Object` (confirmed at `dist/…/master-sync.service.js:364`), neither parameter carries `@Inject()`, and `@Optional()` turns the unresolvable dependency into a silent `undefined` so `:397` returns before doing anything. **Same defect class as `f813b39` (#217)** — a 747-file scan found exactly two silent instances, both here. Only the hand-constructed CLI runner ever worked, so runs 64 and 80 did lifecycle work and 81–113 recorded `{inserted: 0, updated: 0}`. Measured: **4,028 missed departures + 1,130 missed restores = 5,134 contradicting devices**, and **1,050 devices silent >24 h that cannot raise a ticket** because FSM believes they are warehoused. Sliced deliberately: **218a detection ships alone and first**, so the before/after is measured independently rather than self-reported by the sync that was supposed to be doing the work; 218b is the DI fix plus a **DI-booted** test (every existing test hand-constructs the service, which is exactly why CI stayed green); 218c is an operator-gated catch-up window. See `issues/218-lifecycle-drift-detection.md` and `audit/autoplant-reconciliation/`.)*
- 219 — AutoPlant holds 1,251 more NUVISTA vehicles than the ground-truth Excel  *(filed 2026-08-07. Under the authoritative scope (`mst_company` 1004 → `mst_plant` → `mst_vehicle`, ACTIVE plants) production holds 26,465; the Excel holds 25,214. Ruled out by measurement: `vehicle_type` (100% DEDICATED), `vehicle_status` (100% `1`), plant scope (the FSM-derived list was a strict subset with nothing spurious — the authoritative scope *widened* the gap), and the duplicate `NuVista` company record (zero plants). Leading untested hypothesis: the Excel joins through `ap_widgets.tb_vehiclemaster`, so only fitted/pinging vehicles appear. **The only figure in the whole reconciliation that could not be accounted for.** Does not block #218 — every #218 finding is device-level. `corrected-queries.sql` Q7 tests it.)*
- 220 — AutoPlant hard-deletes `mst_vehicle` rows, with no tombstone  *(filed 2026-08-07 from #218 root-cause work. `mst_vehicle` is **not append-only**: of 6,767 departed devices, **1,153 (17.0%) are absent from the table entirely** — checked exhaustively. Looked up by `vehicle_no` instead of `device_id`: **78.9% row hard-deleted** (the `vehicle_no` is gone too), 21.1% device swapped/refitted onto a new `device_id`; 0 of 90 remain in `tb_vehiclemaster`, so they leave telemetry too. Consequence: FSM's mirror freezes — **1,066 devices read `DEPLOYED` in `vehicles.status` for vehicles that no longer exist at source**, which is the concrete case **confirming the #130 three-tier status authority** that the ledger, not the mirror scalar, is lifecycle truth. Judged normal for this source (the 2026-07-17 investigation measured the same class at 3–7%) and filed for durability, not as a defect — the finding otherwise lives only in a reconciliation report.)*
- 221 — Lint the `import type` DI-erasure class + review the 13 `@Optional()` params  *(filed 2026-08-07. Second occurrence after `f813b39`. Audit done: 747 files, exactly 2 silent instances (both #218b), 10 verified false positives — mobile error classes, test doubles, and `SnapshotIngestionWorker` which is factory-provided with an explicit `inject:` array so reflection is never consulted. The class has not spread, but nothing prevents its return. Proposed: a **targeted** rule banning `import type` for constructor-parameter types (not a blanket `consistent-type-imports`, which would be a large noisy change — `import type` is correct everywhere else), plus a justification or `@Inject()` token on each of the 13 `@Optional()` params across 10 files, since `@Optional()` is the amplifier that turned a resolution failure into a fortnight of silence.)*
- 222 — FSM shifts every GPS ping 5.5 h into the past (`AUTOPLANT_UTC_OFFSET_MIN` is wrong) → 223, 228  *(filed 2026-08-07 as reconciliation finding F2; **re-diagnosed 2026-08-07** by the blind PRISM read + `audit/cross-analysis.md`. **The original diagnosis on this issue was wrong and has been withdrawn in place.** It read "not a timezone bug — FSM’s conversion is correct, its data is old"; it is a timezone bug, the conversion is wrong, and the data was fresh. The 5.10 h median came from comparing FSM against the **Excel snapshot** (01:19 UTC) rather than the live source, 25 min before FSM’s own sync (01:45 UTC) — frozen devices give exactly **−5.50 h**, live devices 5.50 − 0:25 = **−5.10 h**, and the whole distribution is 27 min wide. The hard floor the issue itself reported as `min`/`p05` **was the signature**. `07:15 IST − 5:30 = 01:45 IST` — the "staleness" was the shift, to the minute; run 151 wrote 20,578 rows and was not an incomplete pass. **The source never changed either** (my own PRISM claim, also withdrawn): `raw_device_snapshots.gps_datetime` holds the post-conversion value, so per-run percentile lag time-travels the contract — **5.52–5.65 h flat across all 95 runs from 2026-07-07 to 2026-08-07**, no discontinuity. `+330` was wrong when written; the 2026-07-17 verification used fleet-wide `MAX()`, which is decided by **4 devices out of 56,564** that genuinely write IST. Re-measured impact: **434 falsely inactive (16.2% of the queue), all 434 holding an open TROUBLESHOOT ticket**, and 434 of the 665 `CRITICAL` devices fabricated (65% of the band).)*
- 223 — Devices that have never reported are counted as "healthy" → 222, 217, 228  *(filed 2026-08-07 as reconciliation finding F3, independently re-found by the blind PRISM read, extended in `audit/cross-analysis.md`. **P1 DECIDED by operator 2026-08-07: a fitted tracker that has never reported is a fault, not a pipeline state** — counted inactive, ticketed, dispatched. Fleet-wide **913** null-GPS non-departed devices sit in `healthyOperational`, **all 913 also `eligible_for_uptime`** — and since they have no failure cycle they score **100% Fleet Uptime**, the most broken device in the fleet reported as the healthiest. Verified at source: **892 genuinely never reported, 0 UNDEPLOYED**; concentrated in Vasavadatta (545) and Deepak Fertilizer (208). **Why it happens:** the NULL *was* guarded at `device-state.service.ts:108` but never followed through to consumers, and `kpi-definitions.md` then hardened the binary on purpose — *"the identity holds by construction"* — which is the sentence that made a third state unrepresentable. **Starting-point problem RESOLVED:** `tb_vehiclemaster.FIRST_INSTALLED_DATE_TIME` is **100% populated** on the 907 and validates cleanly (predates first ping on 21,238/21,244 reporting devices, 0 bogus); **602 (66%) were fitted over a year ago**. **Correction to the cross-analysis:** ticket creation is a **state scan**, not transition-driven (`ticket-creation.service.ts:34-52`), so the machinery needs **no change** — all 913 already satisfy every precondition and 912 would ticket on the next sweep (+7.3% against 12,571 open). **Ships as one slice with #222** — the two move Fleet Health in opposite directions (82.96% today → 81.90% NDD-only → 85.72% timestamp-only → **84.84% both**). Recommendation recorded: operationally inactive but **reported separately**. P2–P5 unresolved.)*
- 224 — The lifecycle check is not on the Integration Health page → 218  *(filed 2026-08-07 from the `/code-review` Spec axis over the uncommitted #218 work. `IntegrationHealth.lifecycle` ships in the API but `IntegrationHealthView` never picks it up and `BuildHealthPage` never renders it — against `FIX-PLAN.md` §5 "**Placement:** the Integration Health page", in a section titled "the dashboard cannot hide this". **Half the surface is already live and the issue says so**: `ReconciliationPanel.tsx:60` maps all identities generically, so the 9th identity `lifecycleConsistency` renders today with no frontend change; §9 open-question 2 settled on both surfaces and this is the remaining one. - 225 — #218's two documentation deliverables are unwritten → 218  *(filed 2026-08-07 from the `/code-review` Spec axis. `FIX-PLAN.md` §8 named six docs; four were done. - 226 — FSM holds NULL GPS for devices the source has telemetry for → 223  *(filed 2026-08-07 from `audit/cross-analysis.md` §2.1, surfaced while verifying #223’s NDD population against AutoPlant directly. Of the 913 devices FSM holds as null-GPS, **15 have telemetry at the source and 14 pinged within the last 24 h** — FSM stored NULL over a value that exists. Not the NDD question: this is data loss, and the devices are **doubly misreported** (telemetry lost, then the loss counted as health). **15 is only the count we can see** — the measurement covers devices whose FSM value is NULL, so a device stale-but-non-null for the same reason would not appear at all; the true radius is unmeasured, which is the reason to investigate rather than patch. Deliberately **not** diagnosed — candidates listed unranked (`mapVehicleMasterRow` returning `null` and the null persisted, a failed chunk recorded as partial success, cursor skip). First step separates "never read" from "read and dropped" via `raw_device_snapshots`.)*
- 227 — FSM holds device rows the source no longer has → 220, 223  *(filed 2026-08-07 from `audit/cross-analysis.md` §2.1. The **total** `tb_vehiclemaster` orphan count across all 26,543 mirrored devices has not been measured; only the null-GPS slice was checked, so 6 is a floor. Filed separately because folding it into a list inside #223 is exactly how it would be lost, but the disposition decision — retain frozen (the #218 `ABSENT_FROM_READ` posture), tombstone, or exclude — must be **taken once, with #220**, not twice.)*
- 228 — Every guard in this system is one-directional, and each fails toward "fine" → 218, 221, 222, 223, 217  *(filed 2026-08-07 from `audit/cross-analysis.md` §4 — the cross-cutting finding behind three defects found in two days. #218’s `@Optional()` cannot fail on absence; #223’s `healthy + inactive = operational` is a **tautology** (`reconciliation.service.ts:213` says so out loud) and has been green over 913 misclassified devices; #222’s skew guard rejects only the **future** while the bug travelled into the **past**. **Not the same bug** — a DI issue, a modelling gap, a contract error — but one shared failure *mode*: each guard was written against the failure the author imagined and could not fail in the direction the real one travelled. Compounded by a suite that tests the system against its own beliefs (`autoplant-mapping.spec.ts:131` **asserts the wrong constant**, pinning it), and by `MAX()` used as a contract probe — 5 devices out of 56,564 defined the timezone contract for a month, and that trap fired **three times** in one investigation, including on the investigator. **Explicitly NOT "delete checks that cannot fail on data"** — structural guards have real regression value; the defect is that a tautology occupied the slot where a real check belonged, under a label that claimed otherwise, so the remedy is relabel-and-supplement. **Scope widened 2026-08-10 by a fifth specimen (#229, the never-wired auto-recovery sweep): the title says “guard” but the property is broader — what all five share is that non-execution is observationally identical to execution with nothing to do. R3 and R4 already cover it, which is corroboration; R4 widens to “every sweep-shaped service has a registered trigger”.** Four remedies: R4 (boot-time DI resolution test — one file, kills the #217/#218 class, **ship this if only one ships**), R2 (per-run distributional source-contract fingerprints, percentiles never extremes — one assertion would have caught #222 on 2026-07-07), R1 (an **empirical** identity beside each structural one), R3 (typed zeros; the lint half is #221’s, not restated here).)*
- 229 — `AutoRecoveryService.runAutoRecovery` has no production caller; 11,042 open tickets are already closable → 222, 223, 228  *(filed 2026-08-09 while satisfying the operator's pre-application gate on #222 — "tell me the expected closure count for the auto-recovery sweep before it runs." Answering it is what surfaced that **it never runs**: `runAutoRecovery()` has **no `@Cron`, no route, no CLI caller** — its only call site in the repo is `test/auto-recovery.e2e-spec.ts:99`. The single wired entry point is the per-ticket ZM endpoint `POST /tickets/:id/auto-recovery-close`. Eleven other business sweeps have `@Cron` wiring in `business-sweep-scheduler.service.ts`; this one does not. **Confirmed at the data, not inferred:** across 42,955 `ticket_events` transitions — 31,162 `OPEN`, 11,792 `CLOSED`, 1 `CRITICAL_INSERTION_ACCEPTED` — there are **zero `CLOSED_AUTO_RECOVERY`**; the state has never been written. Measured impact: **11,042 of the 12,571 open TROUBLESHOOT tickets (87.8%) already satisfy the sweep's own predicate today**, and **9,888 of them sit on devices that are healthy right now** — the open queue overstates real field work by ~9,888, and that 12,571 baseline is quoted in both #222 and #223. Same failure *mode* as #228: absence of the mechanism and the mechanism finding nothing to do are observationally identical — no log line, no metric, no row — and the green e2e proving the service works is exactly what stops anyone asking whether it is called. **Deliberately NOT bundled into the #222+#223 slice:** the timestamp fix moves this number by **+5** (11,042 → 11,047, measured both over stored and corrected `gps_datetime`), so attributing an 11,042-closure event to #222 would be the same class of error as that issue's withdrawn "not a timezone bug" call. Decisions needed before the first sweep: one-pass vs staged backlog closure (an 88% single-day queue collapse; `CLOSED_AUTO_RECOVERY` keeps it out of SE productivity but not out of the auto-recovery or monthly-uptime metrics), cadence, and whether the 1,154 **flapping** devices — criterion met but inactive *now*, so closing re-opens a cycle on the next creation sweep — should auto-close at all. **Gating posture recorded 2026-08-10: writing the wiring is ordinary work; switching it on is an 11,042-closure event against a 12,571-ticket open book and needs a read-only dry-run, an expected count agreed in advance and written into the issue (the #222 gate, at ~25× the blast radius), and an execution window — land the code disabled.** Also: every rate quoted against the 12,571 baseline in #222 and #223 is diluted ~8× and is now flagged in place — real open work is nearer 1,500. See `issues/229-auto-recovery-sweep-unwired.md`.)*
- 230 — A partial telemetry ingest silently manufactures fleet-wide inactivity; run 153 opened 3,439 phantom tickets → 229, 231, 228, 222  *(filed 2026-08-10 while diagnosing an operator report of "inaccurate data on the dashboard". **[MEASURED]** `POST /integration/run-pipeline` ran snapshot run **153**, which finalised **`PARTIAL` with all 29 chunks `SUCCESS`** — a combination reachable only through the `readError` branch (`snapshot-ingestion.worker.ts:109-118`), i.e. the AutoPlant scan **threw mid-drain** after 29 × 90 = **2,610 rows**. `DeviceStateService.recompute()` then ran across **all 27,032** mirrored devices anyway, so the **24,422 the read never reached** aged on wall-clock alone past the inactivity threshold; ticket creation opened **3,439** Failure Cycles, of which **3,425 (99.6%) are on devices run 153 never fetched telemetry for**. The open book went 12,571 → **15,057** and 15,765 devices now sit 60–120 h "stale" — off a read covering **9.7% of the fleet**. Cross-checked at the source: **19,806 of AutoPlant's 56,588 devices pinged within 6 h**, so a large share of the devices FSM calls inactive are alive. **Explicitly NOT a timezone bug** — 0 future-dated `raw_device_snapshots`, 0 negative `inactivity_hours`, and only **5 of 56,588** source rows sit ahead of `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` (the known IST-writers the #222 skew guard drops; `MAX()` reads 5.5 h ahead purely because of those 5 — the trap #222 documents). Same class as #228: three mechanisms each behaving reasonably compose into a confident wrong answer — the worker finalises `PARTIAL` but **persists the read error nowhere**, recompute has no notion of "this device's evidence is not from this run", and ticket creation cannot know `is_inactive` was fabricated. `data_as_of` advanced to 2026-08-10 13:09 IST on that 9.7% read, so the freshness banner actively reassures during the failure. **Must land before `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED` is turned on**, or a scheduled pipeline repeats this unattended every 30 min. See `issues/230-partial-ingest-manufactures-inactivity.md`.)*
- 231 — `run-pipeline` is an ungated write path; every "gated by `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED`" claim in the repo is only half true → 229, 230, 228, 149  *(filed 2026-08-10. `IntegrationSyncService` has **two** entry points into the same write pipeline: `ingestTelemetry()` behind the `@Cron`'s `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED` (**`false`**), and `runPipeline()` behind `POST /integration/run-pipeline` — an OH role check and **nothing else**. Both run master sync → snapshot → recompute → auto-recovery pre-check → ticket creation, and every stage writes. #229 shipped on the reasoning that its new stage "lands inert because the scheduler flag is false"; **the OH trigger fired the same day and it closed 200 tickets**. Nothing malfunctioned — the gating claim named a *flag* instead of enumerating *callers*, so a second caller predating it by two issues went unconsidered. **What actually bounded the event was `AUTO_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_PASS` (default 200)**, a limit added for "when the operator turns the scheduler on someday": the bound in the code held, the bound in the prose did not. `autoplant-window-preflight.ts` would have reported "✅ ingestion scheduler OFF" ten minutes before run 153. Fix is not "delete the endpoint" — the manual trigger is deliberate and is how first-light and #218c are driven — but that it is **indistinguishable from a safe read at the point of use**: no confirmation, no dry-run, no statement of what it will write, no audit row naming who pressed it. See `issues/231-manual-pipeline-trigger-ungated.md`.)*

Admin suite **456 tests / 95 files** green, `tsc` clean.)  *(filed 2026-08-13, **retrospectively** — the backend was written 2026-08-10 and sat uncommitted and unowned until a status review found it on disk, which is the finding as much as the feature is. Answers an operator ask ("how are newly commissioned devices doing?") whose premise `audit/commissioning-view-feasibility.md` then **corrected**: **96.97% of genuine new installations report within 12–24 h** and the curve is flat after (98.53% at 2–3 days, 95.61% at 3–7 days — sampling noise, not a rising tail), so there is essentially **no commissioning tail** and a device silent past 24 h is not commissioning, it is broken. The measure worth building is therefore **install quality**, not a grace window. Two read-only manager endpoints — `GET /api/reports/commissioning/cohort` and `/installers` — over the rows master sync already appends to `device_commissioning`, joined to `device_states`: **no new module and no new table**, cohort membership derived (`installed_at >= now() - N days`), so nothing moves a device between states and ageing out of the window requires no write. **Online is decided solely by `device_states.first_reported_at`** — `device_commissioning.first_reported_at` is an observation-time snapshot (0 of 24,294 rows populated) and a reader requiring both to agree would report zero commissioned devices forever. **Timing samples exclude fitments before `COMMISSIONING_TTFR_EPOCH`**: the write-once column captured a *last*-seen value for devices already reporting when it landed (15,345 of 23,086 stamped on the single day it shipped, 2,138 stamped earlier than their own `installed_at`, median TTFR ~8,707 h against 17.26 h after). Window ceilings are a **measured** performance contract — bounded shapes hold the index plan at 6.8/9.7 ms (62.7/172.1 ms at 4× the one-year projection) while an unbounded lookback spills the `GROUP BY` to disk at 1,078 ms, and no index fixes it because the cost is the sort forced by `count(DISTINCT …)`. Unresolvable installer logins (17,712 of 24,294) are **labelled, shown, and never ranked** — never silently called a person. Backend verified 35/35 (`commissioning-units` 8, `commissioning-cohort` 27 over the real DI graph and HTTP). **Open, and the reason this is not done: the admin surface.** Two manager-facing endpoints exist that no screen calls — a **parity-gate** item whose deferral reason is not an external-integration blocker. Also open: validating the endpoints against live `fsm` (~24,294 commissioning rows now, fixtures only so far) and the `kpiCatalog`/`kpi-definitions.md` entries. See `issues/232-commissioning-cohort-view.md`.)* **Amended 2026-08-13 — AC-2 was executed and failed (→ #233, fixed); AC-1 then shipped on top of #233 + #234. Drill-through remains #235.**
- 233 — Commissioning cohort counts warehouse devices as failed installs → 232, 176, 217 — **DONE 2026-08-13** (report: `docs/progress/233-commissioning-population.md`; live `fsm` after the fix: **6,810 / 2,655 failed (39.0%) → 2,623 / 136 failed (5.2%)**, census `6,810 = 2,623 operational + 4,187 warehouse + 0 + 0`, 21 ms round trip; the worst installer's never-online rate fell from **100% to 54.5%** — that row was entirely warehouse devices. 135 tests / 7 files green.)  *(filed 2026-08-13 from `audit/recently-commissioned-devices-investigation-2026-08-13.md` §3.2, which is **#232's AC-2 ("validate against live `fsm`") finally being executed**. `gradedSource` (`commissioning-aggregation.service.ts:170`) joins `device_commissioning` to `device_states` with **no operational-fleet predicate**, so a device returned to a warehouse is counted as a failed install. Measured live on `fsm` over the last 90 days: as computed today **6,832 fitments / 2,668 failed = 39.1%**; with `ds.is_departed = false`, **2,645 fitments / 147 failed = 5.6%**. **4,127 of the 6,405 cohort devices (64.4%) are departed.** This is exactly the defect **#176** closed on the dashboard — a numerator that excludes departed devices structurally over a denominator that does not — recreated in a new surface, and unnoticed precisely because no screen calls these endpoints. Fix is a `population` filter defaulting to `operational` (`is_departed = false` + `EXCLUDE_DEACTIVATED_PLANTS`), with `all` preserving today's behaviour as the regression floor; the predicates are **imported** from `dashboard.service.ts` (the `reconciliation.service.ts` precedent) and must never be edited there. **Blast radius zero — nothing consumes these endpoints yet, which is the whole argument for landing this before the page.** See `issues/233-commissioning-cohort-counts-warehouse-as-failed.md`.)*
- 234 — Cohort resolution curve: how fast a fitment batch comes online → 233, 232, 229 — **DONE 2026-08-13** (report: `docs/progress/234-commissioning-resolution-curve.md`; live `fsm`: 83.1% online by 48 h and **flat after**, over `curve=65 (sample 56 + neverOnline 9)` with `preEpochExcluded=2040` and 520 immature of 2,625 fitments; 26 ms at the 90-day ceiling. **AC-4 restated against measurement** — the 97.7% in the issue was New-Installation-only and pre-maturity-gate. 147 tests / 7 files green.)  *(filed 2026-08-13. The operator ask "monitor how their inactivity/status changes over time" has **no trustworthy source today**: `device_states` is overwritten in place, `raw_device_snapshots` is 7-day retained (the 52 surviving partitions are a `PARTITION_MAINTENANCE_ENABLED=false` accident, not a guarantee), `soft_inactive_count_history` is zone-keyed not cohort-keyed, and the obvious `failure_cycles` interval reconstruction is **currently distorted** — measured live, the operational 90-day cohort has **2,183 cycles of which 1,799 (82%) are still OPEN**, because auto-recovery has never executed (**#229**, gated behind `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED`), so a calendar series would rise monotonically as an artefact of the scheduler being off. What IS derivable today from the two stored facts alone is the **fitment-relative resolution curve** — % online by hours since fitment — as `count(*) FILTER (…)` columns on the *same* `GROUPING SETS` pass: no second query, no new endpoint, no new table, no job, no scheduler dependency. Measured live post-epoch (520 samples): **97.7% inside 48 h, 99.6% inside 72 h**, independently confirming the `graceHours = 48` default on FSM's own data rather than on the AutoPlant-side survival curve. The calendar-time series is **deferred with its precondition stated**, not silently dropped. See `issues/234-commissioning-resolution-curve.md`.)*

- 237 — Devices that fail to commission and carry no ticket are invisible to dispatch → 236, 229  *(filed 2026-08-13, a follow-up from #236. A device fitted and never reported may have **no open ticket at all** — measured live, **79 of a 200-row sample (40%)** of `NEVER_REPORTED`-within-90-days devices, 573 such devices server-side total (sample is a lower bound). #236's Assign SE control reads this honestly rather than papering over it, which is what made the gap's size visible for the first time. **Corrected while filing:** the first-draft framing named #229's auto-recovery as the fix; wrong — #229 only *closes* tickets (re-checks staleness on already-open ones), it creates nothing. The mechanism to investigate is `TicketCreationService.createForInactiveEligible`, and whether its eligibility gate covers a device whose `latest_gps_datetime` was never set at all (vs merely stale) is the open question — not resolved here. Needs deciding: whether this is a wiring gap or needs a new `COMMISSIONING_FAILED` ticket origin (SLA, priority, dedup on re-fitment — 6.4% of cohort devices carry more than one fitment in 90 days per #235). - 239 — `X-Acting-As-Zone` is still ignored by ~20 other manager read surfaces → 27, 47  *(filed 2026-08-17 from the operator report "OH dashboard — Act as ZM is non working". Acting had **two** halves and only the audit half was built: `RequestActor` (#47) carries `acted_as_role`/`acting_zone` onto audited writes, but every manager **read** built its scope by hand as `{ role: user.role, zoneId: user.zone_id }` straight off the claims — a shape that structurally cannot carry acting — so an acting OH kept receiving pan-India rows under the Zone Operations Dashboard. The dashboard half is fixed (new `src/common/manager-scope.ts` + `@CurrentScope()`; `DashboardController`'s 9 reads + `ReportsController.fleetUptime`); **`grep -rn "role: user.role, zoneId: user.zone_id" apps/backend/src` went 69 matching lines → 61**, and those 61 across ~20 controllers are this issue. Not a mechanical sweep: each needs the judgement the dashboard needed, some must deliberately **not** narrow (cross-zone escalation exists to span zones), and the **frontend half is per-client too** — `api/devices.ts` and `api/client.ts` still hand-roll bearer-only headers, so a converted backend would never see the header from those pages. Both halves must land together per surface or the change is invisible. Explicitly not the acting **authorization** gate (ROLE_UNAVAILABILITY), which is still Issue 27's own deferred half. See `issues/239-acting-zone-read-scope-remaining-surfaces.md`.)*
- 217 — Operations Data Explorer: one read-only surface that traces any number back to its rows → 176, 160, 121  *(filed 2026-08-06 from an operator ask. A single filterable, Excel-like, **strictly read-only** investigation surface over AutoPlant + FSM master data, operational state, dispatch, tickets, assignments, batches, recommendations and KPI reconciliation — for debugging, reconciliation and audit, explicitly **not** end-user reporting. **Authorization is the operator's ruling and is load-bearing:** gated on `OPERATIONS_HEAD` **and** `OPS_EXPLORER_ENABLED` (default off; disabled ⇒ 404, not 403, so a disabled feature doesn't confirm its own existence) — no sixth RBAC role, because CONTEXT.md:28 is explicit that no Admin persona exists and OH is already the system configurator. The allow-list lives once as `OPS_EXPLORER_ROLES` precisely so a future `PLATFORM_DEVELOPER` is a one-line append rather than a redesign. A second flag `OPS_EXPLORER_DEVELOPER_MODE` (on outside production) adds a **server-side-stripped** lineage layer: source column, SQL expression, endpoint, execution time, raw JSON. Everything is registry-driven — a new dataset is a registry entry, not a new route — and **every identifier emitted into SQL is registry-looked-up while every value is bound**, pinned by test. Export is server-side and streamed, deliberately NOT #160's DOM read, which cannot serve a 20k-row result. Reconciliation reuses #176's `FLEET_COUNT_COLUMNS` rather than respelling the predicates — a second spelling is the exact defect class #176 closed. Slice 1 = engine + `devices` + reconciliation; S2–S5 add datasets, S5 (AutoPlant-side row diff) needs VPN and is last. See `issues/217-operations-data-explorer.md`.)*

- 194 — No committed path seeds a login for the dev database → 91, 133, FE-00 — **DONE 2026-08-13** (report: `docs/progress/194-dev-login-seed-path.md`; runbook: `docs/runbooks/local-development-login.md`. `npm run seed:dev` → `src/seed-dev.ts` → `src/auth/dev-seed.ts`, gated by a **default-off `ALLOW_DEV_SEED`** plus an **unconditional `NODE_ENV=production` refusal the flag cannot override**; `src/seed.ts` untouched — the wall that keeps a real-database seed from minting `*@fsm.test` credentials stands, and this is the *other half*, not a relaxation. Reuses `seedAuthFixtureUsers` rather than defining the accounts twice, so the eight logins a spec uses and the eight a browser uses are the same rows. **Password decision recorded:** the well-known `correct-password` stays the default (requiring an env var would have broken the visual-harness AC on its face, since `manifest.mjs` hardcodes it), overridable via `DEV_SEED_PASSWORD`, blank **rejected** rather than falling back. **Enforced beyond the ACs:** a zone preflight — seeding before `npm run seed` silently wrote ZMs with a null `zone_id`, a login that authenticates then 403s everywhere, worse to diagnose than the 401 this issue removed. **A data-derived third guard was considered and rejected** (the dev DB is an ingested mirror that legitimately looks like production; one real-email admin-UI login would lock a developer out) with the residual risk stated in `dev-seed.config.ts`. 19 tests — 10 pure guard, 4 e2e, +2 on #182's allowlist, +3 preflight; creation-from-empty proven inside a **rolled-back transaction** because `fsm_test` is never truncated (#156) and a real delete would 401 every later file. All three paths run for real against `fsm`: refused / refused-under-production / `Seeded 8 dev login(s)`, which also **retires this machine's hand-run workaround**. Four stale doc sites corrected, one more than the issue listed.)  *(filed 2026-08-04, hit while starting the backend after #193. **`user_credentials` measured empty — 0 rows in the whole table** on the dev DB, so every login 401s for every account and every password, with no diagnostic: `validateCredentials` cannot distinguish "no credential row" from "wrong password" (deliberately — no enumeration oracle). `npm run seed` seeds reference data only (its own docstring says so); `prisma/seed-mock-engineers.ts` creates users with **no** credentials; `seedAuthFixtureUsers` is the only credential minter and its single caller is `test/global-setup.ts` → **`fsm_test`**, never `fsm`. **Caused by #91 S4:** before the in-memory-store retirement, `InMemoryUserStore` seeded five users **at boot**, so `npm start` alone produced working dev logins; S4 deleted that file (verified gone; `auth.module.ts` provides only `PrismaUserStore`) and the accounts survived only by moving into the test-only fixture — the implicit dev-login provision was removed without a replacement. Filed separately rather than parked on #91, whose remainder is scoped to full-suite verification + an optional cookie fast-follow and is `ready-for-human`/HITL. **Reproduces on a clean clone** (migrate → seed → start → all logins 401). Blocks all manual QA behind auth **and `npm run visual:capture`** — FE-00's harness logs in as `ops.head@fsm.test` et al., which exist only in the test DB (likely why `visual/baseline/` has sat stale since 07-28). **NOT related to #190** — none of that bundle touches seeding and `test/global-setup.ts` is committed; committing it would not produce one dev login. Shared theme only (clean clone can't reach a working state); neither blocks the other. Key AC: an idempotent `seed:dev` that **refuses to run against a non-dev database** — the property that justified walling the fixture off, which must not be lost. **SYSTEM-STATE §3j corrected at filing:** it claimed both auth stores are in-memory and that "the dev seed now carries" these accounts; both were false. See `issues/194-no-dev-login-seed-path.md`.)*
- 215 — **✅ DONE 2026-08-20 (`16dbc9c`), with #187.** Nine specs re-zone the shared fixture SE and never clean up; `voucher-controller` is the one that trips over it → 180, 184  *(filed 2026-08-04 after three full sweeps in one session each reported "341 passed, 1 failed" and the correct response each time was "that one doesn't count" — the habit that lets a real regression through. **Not caused by any recent change**, proven by reverting the working tree's backend files to `5afc920` for a byte-identical failure. `seedAuthFixtureUsers` creates the fixture SE's `users` row but **no `engineer_master` row**, and `voucher-controller.e2e-spec.ts` builds no SE fixture of its own — so it depends on what another spec left behind. **Nine** specs (`component-blocked` · `component-request` · `media` · `shadow-use` · `soft-state` · `ticket-forms-read` · `troubleshoot` · `verification-controller` · `verification-review`) each `engineerMaster.upsert` the fixture SE into a **throwaway zone of their own** and not one deletes it; because the upsert is create-only the **first to run wins**, so vitest worker scheduling — invisible from the failing test — decides which zone the shared fixture SE is in for the rest of the run. Alone → 400 `SE_NOT_FOUND`, 3 fail; after a spec that re-zoned it → the ZM-North queue is zone-scoped, 2 fail. Reproduced deliberately by running `verification-controller` immediately before it and watching the failure change shape from 3 to exactly the 2 seen in the full sweep. **Completes #180**: truncate-and-reseed made the DB deterministic at the *start* of a run; this is the hole it left, state mutated *during* one. **Order trap recorded on the issue:** fixing the leak alone makes `voucher-controller` fail harder (always 3), so the voucher fixture comes first or both land together. Nothing in `src/` is at fault — the zone scoping is correct. See `issues/215-shared-fixture-se-zone-leak.md`.)*

- 147 — SE day-plan date filter + `work_schedules` lifecycle closure → 11  *(**done 2026-08-04**, both slices. **Slice 1:** `getDayPlan` gained `dateFrom <= today <= dateTo` (via the shared `common/utc-day.ts`), driven by the issue's own three-case truth table — case (c), yesterday's schedule carrying the *newer* `dispatchedAt`, failed for a different reason than the empty case and would have survived a naive fix. Signature is now `getDayPlan(seId, { now? })` so fixtures state their clock; `schedules.controller.ts` unchanged. **Slice 2:** `ScheduleClosureScheduler` (`PlantEligibilityRefreshScheduler` shape — `BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED`, default OFF, `SCHEDULE_CLOSURE_CRON` 04:00 UTC, single-in-flight, structured outcome) writes `COMPLETED`/`PARTIAL` onto past-dated rows; **no schema change** — both terminal values already existed and were already outside `LIVE_SCHEDULE_STATUSES`. Terminal value is *derived* (any still-assigned unresolved ticket ⇒ `PARTIAL`), not guessed. The APPEND race is closed by taking the **same `dispatch_zone_<id>` advisory lock** #127 holds, extracted to `dispatch-zone-lock.ts` so two hand-spelled keys can't silently take different locks; test holds it from a second connection and proves the zone is skipped, then collected next tick. Wiring proved by its own spec (every behavioural test builds the service by hand and would miss an unregistered provider). Recorded trade-off: contention runs both ways — a dispatch that loses the lock skips the zone for that run, held off by hour-apart crons + one short tx per zone. Report: `docs/progress/147-day-plan-date-filter-schedule-closure.md`. **Five sibling reads with the same missing predicate found and filed as #191** — Slice 2 fixes them by construction, but only while the closer is enabled.)*

See `issues/159-plant-ranking-recommender-config.md`.)*
- 148 — Business-sweep staleness precondition → 108  *(filed 2026-07-22 from full-project-audit B4. Sweeps run every 5 min (`.env:38` `BUSINESS_SWEEPS_ENABLED="true"`) and expire 24 h windows on **wall-clock**, while `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED="false"` means nothing automatically writes pings — so a submission ages into an **irreversible** `FAILED_VERIFICATION` (+ `PRE_VERIFICATION` inventory rollback) regardless of whether the SE fixed the device. Gate on `snapshot_runs.data_as_of` advancing past `submittedAt`; strictly more conservative, needs no ops discipline. **Do not** auto-expire after a grace period — that re-creates the bug with a longer fuse; surface stalled windows on `/build-health` instead. Same coupling class the activation checklist already documents for ingestion↔partition-maintenance and enforces for neither.)*

Two blocking questions: **Q1** does the business ever rank the same customer differently per zone — if not, per-zone is scope creep and the feature collapses to UI copy; **Q2** ranking must actually move the engine, so either grow scope so it does (note: `canonical-sort.ts:53-54` is gated by **no** weight, unlike the scoring seam) or reframe honestly as "customer tiebreaker ordering" — the **weight-0 middle path was proposed and then vetoed** as the silent-lie pattern of run-65/#130. **Q3** (cross-feature, only live if Q1 = per-zone): does a #158 zone change re-attach per-zone priorities silently or behind a warning? #158 S2 was built without that warning on the standing assumption Q1 = global.)*

## Mobile (foundation + M-series UI)

The RN/Expo SE app shell and the mobile UI surfaces for backend issues whose mobile halves were
deferred (04/07/11/12/15/16/18/21). **Mobile Foundation (54) blocks every M-series issue.** Until 54
lands, all mobile ACs across the backlog are `blocked-by #54`, never silently deferred. See
`DEV-GOVERNANCE-CHANGE-SET.md` Part 3.3.

> **#54 — built 2026-08-04, one AC open (HITL).** Bottom-tab shell (Home/Tickets/Stock/Vouchers/
> Profile), 6 component-kit primitives, offline-aware client (`WriteQueue` + NetInfo connectivity,
> in-memory — durable persistence is #17), `X-Device-Id`+`X-App-Version` on every request incl.
> login/refresh, `/api/v1` base path, `app.json` real bundle ids. **M-series is unblocked as of this
> commit.** Not built: the update-required screen's trigger — #170's backend half (server
> min-version floor + a distinct error code) does not exist, and the exact code/shape is a
> permanent (no-OTA) contract decision, not built under AFK authorization. See #54's own
> 2026-08-04 comment and #170.

> **Mobile TDD-readiness normalization (audit 2026-06-29):** every mobile issue (17, 20, 54–61, 63,
> 64, 66, 68, 71, 77, 85–89) now carries the TDD specification standard — **API contract**, **validation
> & error codes**, **permissions**, **navigation**, **offline behaviour**, **edge cases**, and **test
> targets** — with every contract pinned to the verified backend on the integrated line. Business logic
> was documented, not changed. Backend gaps surfaced by the audit are owned by **#81–#84** (below);
> they are no longer hidden inside mobile issues. See `docs/archive/HANDOFF-2026-06-29-mobile-tdd-prep.md`.

| 2026-08-05 | **[#209](./issues/209-mobile-build-pipeline-device-install.md) — the mobile app has a native Android build for the first time; NOT yet on a handset, so the issue stays open.** `./gradlew assembleDebug` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, `app-debug.apk` 58.4 MB, arm64-v8a, 22 native libs. **Autolinking resolved everything, including `react-native-keychain` and `react-native-community_netinfo`** — the biggest unknown in the plan. Whether the keychain *works* is still unproven: `adb devices` was empty for the whole session, so the two ACs carrying the actual value (installs+launches; `se.north@fsm.test` logs in and the session survives an app restart) are **blocked on hardware**, not deferred. **The build fight, because it will recur on any new machine:** five failures each hiding the next, and the root cause was not path length as it first appeared — **Android CMake 3.22.1 bundles ninja 1.10, whose manifest lacks `longPathAware`**, so it is hard-capped at 260 chars regardless of the registry. Fix is `sdkmanager "cmake;3.31.6"` (ninja 1.12.1, long-path aware), pinned in `android/build.gradle`. Path shortening alone provably cannot work: **175 of the 319 characters are immovable React Native codegen structure**, so even `nodeLinker: hoisted` + a 2-char repo root lands ~272. Kept as margin (each independently justified): `LongPathsEnabled=1` (machine registry, admin — **cannot live in the repo, so a fresh clone without it fails identically**), `-DCMAKE_OBJECT_PATH_MAX=1024`, `buildStagingDirectory → C:/x/<module>`, `virtualStoreDir: C:/pv` + `virtualStoreDirMaxLength: 40` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (chosen over `nodeLinker: hoisted` to keep pnpm's isolated linking, so phantom deps still fail loudly in backend/admin), and `reactNativeArchitectures=arm64-v8a` (armeabi-v7a is 2 chars longer and overflowed; also ~4x faster, and `expo run:android` builds one ABI anyway). Two dead ends recorded in-file so nobody retries them: relocating modules' Gradle `buildDir` (RN's `Android-autolinking.cmake` reads the *project* dir, not the build dir) and raising `CMAKE_OBJECT_PATH_MAX` alone. **`apps/mobile/android/` is now TRACKED**, deliberately against Expo's CNG advice — the `build.gradle` block can't be a config plugin and would be silently wiped; `expo prebuild --clean` is banned in the runbook. Also landed: netinfo **12.0.1 → 11.4.1 pinned exactly** (backs `connectivity.ts`, which #186 depends on), expo 54.0.35 → 54.0.36, `expo install --check` clean; a root `ErrorBoundary` (TDD, 6 tests) mounted **outside** `AuthProvider` so a keychain-rehydrate throw is caught; `.env.example` + runbook covering both networking routes — `adb reverse` (recommended: no LAN IP, no firewall rule) and LAN IP (**verified no inbound rule for TCP 3000 exists and all three profiles default inbound to Block**, so that route would fail as a timeout resembling a wrong URL). Mobile suite 47 suites / 319 tests green; `tsc` clean. **Raised, not folded in:** `android.permission.CAMERA` is absent from the generated manifest while `InstallFormScreen.tsx:43` and `VoucherFormScreen.tsx:55` call `launchCameraAsync` (`expo-image-picker` missing from `app.json` plugins) — photo capture will fail on device; **needs a follow-up issue**. Runbook: `docs/runbooks/mobile-android-build.md`. | `fd289de` |
| 2026-08-05 | **[#216](./issues/216-mobile-camera-permission-missing-from-manifest.md) filed — `android.permission.CAMERA` is absent from the generated manifest, so BOTH photo flows are dead on a handset.** Found by [#209](./issues/209-mobile-build-pipeline-device-install.md)'s first native build and deliberately not fixed there. `expo-image-picker` is missing from `app.json`'s `plugins`, so its config plugin never injects the permission — and **Android will not grant a runtime permission the manifest never declared**, so `requestCameraPermissionsAsync()` returns `granted: false` with no system dialog at all. Both call sites then bail to a bare `CAMERA_PERMISSION_DENIED` with no in-app recovery (`VoucherFormScreen.tsx:48-52`, `InstallFormScreen.tsx:38-42`). Invisible to Jest and `tsc` — every test mocks `expo-image-picker`, and the manifest is generated rather than authored. **This contradicts `audit/mobile-device-readiness-2026-08-04.md` in two places** (line 64 "Voucher and Install photos work"; line 159 Vouchers is "**the one screen you can exercise for real today**") — the audit read the source correctly but predates any APK, so it could not have checked the manifest. #216 therefore owns the audit correction as an AC, not just the one-line `app.json` fix, because the pilot readiness picture currently rests on that claim. | (uncommitted — filed with this commit) |

| 2026-08-05 | **Home dashboard brought up to `docs/ui/mobile/home-dashboard.png` — the missing chart is real now, not deferred ([#175](./issues/175-se-work-history-series.md) half-closed, [#55](./issues/55-mobile-home.md) parity).** Operator ask: "elements are missing like graphs". Built the one piece #172 decision 1 had explicitly shipped Home without. **Backend — `GET /api/me/work-history?days=N`** (`me-work-history.service.ts`, provider on `MeTicketsModule`, route on the existing `MeTicketsController`): dense oldest-first `[{date, assigned, completed}]`, `days` clamped 1–31 because a query value is an unvalidated string and a junk one must render a default chart, not 500 a working screen. **The source decision #175 said to settle before building:** assigned(D) = distinct tickets in the SE's batches on every schedule covering **IST** day D minus ZM-removed rows; completed(D) = of *that day's assigned set*, tickets with a `ticket_events` closure row inside day D. Read from the **event ledger, not `tickets.status`** — the status column says where a ticket ended up, never when, so "what did this SE complete on 09 May" is unanswerable from it once the ticket moves again; the ledger is also what a ZM's view of the same day reads, which is the AC that the two agree. `completed ⊆ assigned` is therefore true **by construction**, which is what lets a bar print `4/6` as a fraction and never overflow its track. Closure states are byte-identical to the KPI strip's COMPLETED tile — same screen, so a second definition would be visible as a contradiction. Spec pins the IST boundary with a closure at `2026-06-18T18:45Z` (00:15 IST on the 19th) that scores **0 under UTC bucketing**; also pins that the fixture cannot put one ticket live in two batches (partial-unique `(ticket_id) WHERE removed_at IS NULL`). **Mobile —** `WorkHistoryChart` (7 bars, `completed/assigned` label, date axis, two-series legend), `PlantWorkloadCard` (ring + meter + work/pending legend + ratio), `plantSummary.ts` (one derivation feeding **both** the Next-Visit subline and the workload cards, so the two cards cannot disagree about the same plant), branded header block with the Online / Last-sync chips. **Charts are plain Views on purpose:** `react-native-svg` ships native code, so adding it invalidates the installed debug APK and forces the [#209](./issues/209-mobile-build-pipeline-device-install.md) Android rebuild — it buys nothing a stacked rounded view can't draw. Re-reading the reference settled that the workload **ring is a static badge, not a gauge** (drawn identically on its 50% and 0% cards); the horizontal bar under it is the meter. **Two image elements deliberately NOT built, both already-owned:** the `ID - ANV1012` employee code (#55's superseding 2026-08-03 ruling — no source exists and a UUID under someone's name is worse than none) and the `Scan` FAB (QR scanner = **#20**, already recorded in SYSTEM-STATE §4.4.3; needs `expo-camera`, i.e. a native module and a full rebuild, not a screen change). **Verified on the handset**, not just in jest: `adb reverse` + `se-z5-15@mock.fsm`, chart live at `0/25 0/25 0/25 … 0/21` over 30 Jul–05 Aug, six workload cards, Next Visit `5 inactive · 1 urgent · 0 in work`. Backend rebuilt and restarted to register the route. Mobile 49 suites / 334 tests, `tsc` + eslint clean; backend `tsc` clean, 19 tests across the three `me-tickets` specs. | `57dfb07` `a4ab238` |

| 2026-08-06 | **[#217](./issues/217-operations-data-explorer.md) filed and Slice 1 built — the Operations Data Explorer: one read-only surface that traces any number back to its rows.** Operator ask for a single filterable, Excel-like source of truth spanning AutoPlant + FSM masters, operational state, dispatch, tickets, assignments, batches, recommendations and KPI reconciliation — for debugging/reconciliation/audit, explicitly **not** end-user reporting, and never able to write. **The authorization shape is the operator's ruling and is the load-bearing part:** gated on `OPERATIONS_HEAD` **and** `OPS_EXPLORER_ENABLED` (default off everywhere; disabled ⇒ **404, not 403**, so a diagnostic tool that is off does not confirm its own existence), with **no sixth RBAC role** — CONTEXT.md:28 is explicit that no Admin persona exists and OH is already the system configurator. The allow-list lives once as `OPS_EXPLORER_ROLES`, which is precisely what makes a future `PLATFORM_DEVELOPER` a one-line append rather than a redesign; an e2e test asserts no handler hard-codes a role string, and another asserts the module registers no mutating verb. A second flag `OPS_EXPLORER_DEVELOPER_MODE` (on outside production) adds the lineage layer — source column, SQL expression, formula, endpoint, per-query timings, bound parameters, raw JSON — **stripped server-side**, so with it off those fields are absent from the payload rather than hidden in the browser. Both flags are read **per request**, unlike the cron schedulers that resolve once at decorator evaluation: an operator turning the tool on mid-incident should not have to bounce the process, and the e2e spec pins that by flipping the env against one booted app. **The engine is registry-driven** — `dataset-registry.ts` declares each dataset's FROM/JOIN and every column's full provenance, so a new dataset is a registry entry, not a new route — and the **injection boundary is that registry lookup**: a request names a column by `key`, the pure builder resolves it or 400s `UNKNOWN_COLUMN`, the SQL emitted is the code-authored fragment, and every value is bound (`Prisma.join` for `in`, LIKE metacharacters escaped, `eq`/`neq` vs null routed to `IS [NOT] NULL`, `neq` as `IS DISTINCT FROM`, empty `in` as an explicit `FALSE`). That builder is **DB-free and carries 36 unit tests on purpose** — per #156 the e2e suite is not a reliable green/red signal, and these are the properties that must not regress. Export is **server-side and streamed**, deliberately NOT #160's `TableDownloadButton` DOM read: that mechanism is correct for an operational table (it is the zone-scoping proof) but structurally cannot export past the rendered page, and this is the app's only server-paged table. **Reconciliation** evaluates five identities over the whole live database — zone roll-up and company roll-up against the KPI strip, plus the three structural partitions — reporting both sides, *how each side was measured*, the signed difference (direction matters: `+12` and `-12` have different causes) and ranked candidate explanations; it **imports** `FLEET_COUNT_COLUMNS`/`EXCLUDE_DEACTIVATED_PLANTS` from `dashboard.service.ts` (both newly exported) rather than restating the predicates, because a checker written from a second spelling only verifies that the second spelling agrees with itself — the exact defect class #176 closed. Slice 1 ships the `devices` dataset; S2–S5 (other FSM datasets, then an AutoPlant-side source-vs-FSM row diff behind the VPN) are open on the issue. Verified: backend **36 unit + 16 e2e green** and the 14 pre-existing `dashboard-kpi-reconciliation` tests still green; admin **94 files / 440 tests green**; both apps `tsc --noEmit` clean. One flake observed and chased down rather than accepted: `tickets-list.test.tsx` failed on one full admin run and passed in isolation, alongside my new spec, and on a clean re-run of all 94 files — not caused by this change, and consistent with the cross-file interference #215/#184 describe on the backend. SYSTEM-STATE §3k updated in place; both flags documented in `.env.example`. | `ba6053c` |
| 2026-08-06 | **[#217](./issues/217-operations-data-explorer.md) Slice 2 — the Operations Data Explorer grows from 1 dataset to 11, on the unchanged S1 engine.** Ten new registry entries — `zones`, `companies`, `plants`, `vehicles` (FSM + AutoPlant master data), `engineers`, `tickets`, `batches`, `dispatchRuns`, `recommendations`, `auditLogs` (operational state, dispatch, audit trail) — added with **zero new routes, controllers, or pages**: every dataset inherits filtering/search/sort/pagination/export/reconciliation/Developer-Mode from the S1 registry-driven engine by construction. **Found and fixed a real S1 bug while extending it, rather than shipping it eleven times over:** `devices.plantName`'s drilldown route needed the plant's numeric id, but the column DISPLAYS `plants.name` — `:value` was silently substituting the plant's name into an id-taking URL. `devices.deviceId`'s drilldown target didn't even read a `deviceId` query param — `DeviceDetailPage.tsx`'s URL contract is zoneId/companyId/plantId/bucket/status only, confirmed by reading the page, not assumed from the route's name. Fixed with a new `DatasetColumn.drilldown.valueSql`: an optional companion SQL expression, code-authored like every other registry fragment, projected as a hidden `__dd_<key>` row field alongside the visible display value; the FE builds the link href from `row['__dd_'+key] ?? row[key]`, falling back to the shown value when no companion was declared (the common case — a ticket's UUID is both shown and linked). `serializeDataset` strips `valueSql` in **both** modes, not just operational, since it's raw SQL like everything else the function already strips — caught by a new test, not shipped, since the strip was initially only a docstring promise. `deviceId`'s broken drilldown was removed outright (no single-device deep link exists anywhere in this app today) rather than left pointing at a dead end; `zoneName`/`companyName` on `devices` gained real drilldowns as a side effect of building the fix. Every new dataset's drilldown targets an **existing** route (`/schedules/:engineerId`, `/tickets/:ticketId`, `/batches/:batchId`, `/dispatch-runs/:runId`, `/reports/device?{zoneId,companyId,plantId}=`), each confirmed by reading the target page's actual param-reading code; `auditLogs` deliberately gets **no** drilldown — `entityType` varies row to row with no single safe target, and a wrong link is worse than none in a tool built on "click through to the real rows". **Reconciliation gained a 6th identity, `dispatchBatchLedger`** (`Σ dispatch_runs.batches = COUNT(plant_batch_assignments) WHERE run_id IS NOT NULL`), added specifically because the batches/dispatchRuns datasets now exist to make it meaningful — deliberately NOT a ticket-count identity, since `batch_assignment_tickets.removed_at` reflects legitimate ZM overrides and would make a ticket-level check chronically (and correctly!) FAIL, training operators to ignore the panel; batch ROWS are never deleted, only re-statused, so the run ledger's own counter against surviving rows is a genuinely stable structural invariant, distinct in kind from the five device-population identities. Verified end-to-end with a one-off probe spec (written, run, then deleted) that hit query+export for all 11 datasets against the live schema — not just registry shape checks, actual SQL execution. Final tally: backend unit **44 (was 36)** + e2e **16** green, the pre-existing `dashboard-kpi-reconciliation` **14** still green, admin **94 files / 441 tests** green, both apps `tsc --noEmit` clean. Issue file updated in place with full S2 notes; SYSTEM-STATE §3k updated in place. S3 (AutoPlant source-vs-FSM row diff, needs VPN) remains open and is the last slice by design. | `ba6053c` |
| 2026-08-06 | **[#217](./issues/217-operations-data-explorer.md) closed — Slice 3 re-scoped from "browse AutoPlant rows" to "reuse AutoPlant's existing reconciliation", on the operator's sign-off.** The issue originally planned S3 as a 12th browsable dataset live-reading AutoPlant MySQL row-by-row, matching the shape of the other 11. Building it surfaced a better fit: `AutoPlantHealthService.reconciliationHealth()` (`ingestion/autoplant/health.service.ts:219`) **already performs exactly this comparison** — a live source-vs-FSM row-COUNT diff for plants and vehicles, already behind `/api/integration/health`, built for review A6/Issue 97 Slice 5 — and a bulk per-row browsable dataset would have duplicated it while risking the **DBA's <100-row-per-query cap** that every other AutoPlant read in this codebase (master sync included, ≤90 rows/query) respects; a live filterable UI table over that source is not a shape that fits the cap. Offered the tradeoff to the operator directly rather than silently picking one; they chose reuse. Implementation: made `reconciliationHealth()` public (was `private`) and injected it into the Explorer's `ReconciliationService` by having `OpsExplorerModule` import `IngestionModule` — **not** a local re-`useFactory`'d instance, which would have been the exact "silent duplicate singleton" #105 already documents elsewhere (`recommender.module.ts`/`engineers.module.ts`); importing the owning module for its exported provider is the fix #105 itself prescribes, applied rather than repeated. Added a third `IdentityStatus`, `UNAVAILABLE`, distinct from `FAIL`: "could not be checked" (no VPN, unconfigured env) is not the same claim as "checked and disagrees", and `ReconciliationReport.status` only flips to FAIL on an actual mismatch, never on UNAVAILABLE alone — otherwise every dev/test/CI box would report the whole panel as broken. FE renders UNAVAILABLE neutrally (no red, no fabricated left/right comparison, just the reason), matching the app's existing info-affordance conventions. Verified **deterministically, not with a mock**: `test/setup-env.ts`'s env allowlist (#182) deletes every `AUTOPLANT_MYSQL_*` var for the whole e2e suite, so "AutoPlant unconfigured" is the actual, reproducible state of every test run; the new e2e spec asserts directly against that fact. Reconciliation panel: **8 identities** (was 6) — `autoplantPlantsCount`/`autoplantVehiclesCount` reuse the SAME `AutoPlantMasterSource` count methods the health endpoint already calls, never a second AutoPlant query implementation. | 2026-08-07 | **[#218](./issues/218-lifecycle-drift-detection.md) slice b — the DI fix, proved on the production graph.** Value imports **plus** explicit `@Inject()` tokens on `departures`/`floatingEligibility`; `@Optional()` kept (the CLI runner and several specs construct the service with fewer arguments). No cycle introduced — checked first, not assumed: `master-mapping.ts` is a pure leaf with zero imports and nothing in `org/` reaches this module. **Correction to my own Stage-1 plan, settled by experiment rather than argument:** the plan called dropping `type` "the one-line fix" and the token "redundant" — **backwards**. The declared type is a union (`T | null`), and TypeScript erases *any* union to `Object` in `design:paramtypes` independently of import style, so I built the intermediate variant (value imports, no `@Inject`) and it was **still 4/4 red**. The token is what makes Nest resolve; the value import is what stops that token being `undefined`; the third combination (`import type` + `@Inject`) does not compile, which is the only loud failure mode of the three. Both required, neither sufficient — recorded on the constructor so the next reader does not "tidy" the token away. New spec `master-sync-di-wiring.e2e-spec.ts` boots the **real `AppModule`** — the same graph the scheduler and `POST /api/integration/sync` resolve through — and overrides **only** `MASTER_SYNC_SOURCE` (unreachable from CI); a hand-constructed service here would have re-created the exact blind spot that hid the bug. It asserts both collaborators resolve, then drives DEPLOYED → UNDEPLOYED → DEPLOYED and checks the departure row opens and closes with history intact. **Red-green run in both directions:** pre-fix 4/4 red with the behavioural failures reproducing the production signature `{inserted: 0, updated: 0}` verbatim; value-imports-only 4/4 red; fixed 4/4 green. Regression **67/67 across 9 files**, including the pre-existing 8-test `device-departure-lifecycle` and 14-test `dashboard-kpi-reconciliation` suites; `tsc --noEmit` clean. **No production data written** — the dev-DB reading is byte-identical to the 218a baseline (`drift 5134 · missingFromSource 1131 · quietRuns 27`), confirming the fix changes future syncs only and applies no catch-up by itself. **218c (the catch-up window: ~4,030 departures + ~1,130 restores, 3,709 tickets force-closed, 1,461 on live batches) remains operator-gated and NOT approved.** | `9c00ad6` |
