Still no production data written; 218c remains operator-gated and unapproved.** (1) **The notification-seam assertion is now programmatic** (`npm run autoplant:window-preflight`, wired into FIX-PLAN §7.5 as step 4). It boots the **real `AppModule`** and asserts the bound `NOTIFICATION_CHANNEL_GATEWAY` is still the inert `LoggingChannelGateway` *and* returns `UNAVAILABLE` on all four external channels, plus re-verifies `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED` at the moment it matters. **Identity is checked before behaviour, and that ordering is the safety property** — an unrecognised gateway is rejected *without being invoked*, because probing an unknown adapter to discover whether it sends would itself be the send the gate exists to prevent; a spec pins that with a gateway that counts touches, and another pins the same-class-changed-behaviour case with a subclass, since a name check alone would pass it. Exit code is the contract; **both paths exercised for real** — clean run exits 0, and forcing `INGESTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=true` exits 1 with `DO NOT RUN THE CATCH-UP WINDOW`. This replaces a by-hand check that expires silently the day real adapters land. (2) **The live-batch stand-down list is exported and exact.** `--export-standdown <path>` on the existing read-only dry-run rather than a second command: the export needs the departure plan, the plan needs the full ~26k-row source read, and that read is capped at 90 rows/query by the AutoPlant DBA — a separate tool would cost a second full read *and* let the two disagree across ~150 vehicles/day of churn. Deriving both from one read is also what **pins the absence cohort exactly instead of the ~300 estimated at Gate 3: 1,691 live-batch tickets across 234 batches = 1,395 `SOURCE_STATUS` + 296 `ABSENT_FROM_READ`** (the 1,461 on record was the SOURCE_STATUS cohort alone). The row builder imports `TERMINAL_TICKET_STATUSES` from `device-departure.service` rather than respelling the predicate, so the exported set is by construction the set the window closes — the same anti-drift move 218a made with `lifecycleHealth()`. (3) **SE-team note drafted** for the operator to send (`audit/autoplant-reconciliation/SE-TEAM-NOTE.md`) — carries the exact closure signature (`closure_type = 'DEVICE_UNDEPLOYED_CLOSE'`) so the team can isolate the whole set from a query, and flags that closure is one-way and batch rows are untouched. **Second read-only dry-run against production AutoPlant confirms the Gate-3 figures are stable**: 5,238 departures vs 5,240 (UNDEPLOYED 3,886 · MISSING_FROM_SOURCE 1,306 · MAINTENANCE 46), 4,382 tickets vs 4,383 — the ±1–2 is fleet churn across the ~8 h between reads, not instability. **Two figures in `FIX-PLAN.md` §7.1 are superseded and now marked as such** (3,709 tickets closed → 4,383; backlog landing ~14,100 → ~13,400): both predate the discovery that the forecast was structurally blind to the absence class. New stat `entity_stats.commissioning` — safe because every consumer reads named JSON paths, none enumerate keys. **(2) The three vacuous passes are now real.** "no-op on re-sync" and "no NULL duplication" were `0 === 0` and `n === n` against a table nothing wrote to; both now carry non-empty preconditions (the NULL test asserts a NULL row was *actually written* before asserting it does not multiply — without that it passes with the modifier dropped, which is the whole point of the test). "Inert on failure" was the most vacuous — `status === 'SUCCESS'` was true of a sync that never touched the table — and is now the strongest. **A real trap found and encoded:** `vi.spyOn(prismaDelegate, …).mockRestore()` DELETES the own property vitest installed and does not revive the Prisma proxy behind it, so every later call throws "createMany is not a function" — which reads exactly like an appender bug. Caused one false failure here; the restore is now explicit. **(3) `first_reported_at` has coverage for the first time** — new `snapshot-first-reported-dualwrite.e2e-spec.ts`, 7 tests: write-once against a later ping **and against an earlier one** (COALESCE not LEAST — after #222 correct values are 5.5 h *later*, so a LEAST would pin every device to its pre-fix value forever), chunk-**min** not chunk-max, offset 0 recorded, a null corrected timestamp leaving the column unset rather than freezing a `+330` value, and the 5.5 h inversion pinned **deliberately** as a tripwire to be deleted when #222 lands. | 2026-08-09 | **#222 + #223 shipped as one slice — the fleet-health state model corrected end to end, and the operator's pre-application gate answered with a number nobody expected.** **The gate first.** The operator required the expected auto-recovery closure count *in the issue before the sweep runs*. Root cause: **the sweep has no production caller at all** (no `@Cron`, no route, no CLI — only a spec), confirmed at the data by **zero `CLOSED_AUTO_RECOVERY` rows in 42,955 `ticket_events`**. The past arm is a **sentinel floor (year 2000)**, and #222's proposed fleet-p99 past guard was **declined and the reasoning recorded**: a ping older than p99 is not implausible data, it is the finding — catching a systematic shift needs #228's R2 distributional check, which this is not. **#223:** three-state model `operational = healthy + inactive + neverReported`, both rates over the new **`reportingOperational`** denominator (P3 — excluded, not scored 0%); | 2026-08-10 | **[#230](./issues/230-partial-ingest-manufactures-inactivity.md) guard implemented — an incomplete telemetry read can no longer drive a single downstream write.** **The design changed on one measurement, and the reason is the finding.** #230 item 1(a) proposed passing the run's covered `device_id` set into `recompute()`. **Unsound here:** `SnapshotIngestionService.ingestChunk` writes `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })` against a `(device_id, gps_datetime)` UNIQUE, so a device that *was read* but has not pinged since produces **no row for that run**. "Has a ping row for run N" means **new ping**, not **was read** — a per-device coverage filter built on it would refuse to age exactly the genuinely-silent devices the platform exists to catch, i.e. wrong in the dangerous direction. *(This also refines yesterday's phrasing: the "3,425 tickets on devices never fetched" join measures devices with no **new ping row**; the hard coverage bound is the chunk arithmetic — 29 × 90 = 2,610 of 56,588 — which stands independently.)* **So the gate is the run's own SUCCESS/PARTIAL verdict**, which was already correct and already recorded; what was missing was anyone *consulting* it before writing. **Shipped:** `DeviceStateService.recompute(now, trigger, { skipDerivation })` — step 1 (ensure a row per device) still runs because that is not a derivation and cannot be wrong, while the derive UPDATE, ledger and canary are skipped so `inactivity_hours`/`is_inactive`/`sla_bucket`/`computed_at` keep last-good values; returns `{ upserted, derived }`. New shared `IntegrationSyncService.runPostIngestStages()` sets `ingestComplete = snapshot.status === 'SUCCESS'` and, when false, skips derivation **and** the auto-recovery pre-check **and** ticket creation with a loud WARN — **shared by `ingestTelemetry()` and `runPipeline()` deliberately**, since gating only the cron would have prevented nothing when [#231](./issues/231-manual-pipeline-trigger-ungated.md)'s ungated OH trigger is the path that fired. `PipelineSummary.ingestComplete` is a typed field, not a log line (#228 R3). **`computed_at` lagging is the point:** stale-but-true beats fresh-and-fabricated, and the staleness becomes visible rather than inferred. **Tests (6 new):** PARTIAL and FAILED refuse on both entry points, SUCCESS still runs everything so the gate is not a blanket off-switch, and the device-state case advances the clock **500 h** under `skipDerivation`, asserts the device does **not** go inactive, then re-runs the same clock **without** the guard and asserts it does — so it cannot pass vacuously. **#230 item 5 measured:** the reader's exact keyset scan replicated read-only **completes cleanly — 629 pages, 56,591 rows, 1,289 s (21.5 min)** — so run 153's abort was **transient, not deterministic** (no poison row). But pages 1–29 run at **4.8 s/page** and ~12 s/page around page 30, against **0.375 s/page** by page 600: **run 153 died inside the slowest region, 10–30× slower than the tail**, making a query/connection timeout the leading hypothesis. Recommended there: an explicit logged query timeout (so the failure names itself instead of surfacing as a bare `PARTIAL`), read-side retry with backoff (chunk *writes* already get 3 attempts; a single *read* throw ends the whole scan), and resumable continuation from the last scanned `device_id`. Also noted: 56,591 rows vs 56,588 minutes earlier — the source churns during the 21-min scan, so a run is never a consistent fleet snapshot. **Still open:** the **3,439 cycles already opened** by run 153 are untouched — the guard prevents recurrence, it does not undo — plus #230 items 2 (persist `read_error`/coverage, needs a migration) and 4 (`data_as_of` still advances on a partial read, so the freshness banner still reassures during the failure). **One test-isolation bug found and fixed on the way, and it is NOT this slice's doing:** a full run failed `fleet-uptime-report` with `expected 5 to be 3` on `fleet.eligibleDeviceCount` — an assertion made at **fleet scope, i.e. unfiltered by zone**, so it counts every `device_downtime_summary_monthly` row for its month whoever wrote it. It used **May 2026**, the same month as `fleet-uptime-aggregation.e2e-spec.ts`, whose `computeMonth(MONTH)` is a **global delete+insert across all devices**; when the two interleave across workers the aggregation's rows land in the month the other is counting. Two hypotheses were tested and **both were wrong** before the real one — first the #229 fixture's `eligibleForUptime` (changed to `false` anyway: `runAutoRecovery` never reads that column, so 5 devices were sitting in the scope of every unscoped "all eligible" assertion for nothing), then this slice's far-future `recompute()` (irrelevant — the spec reads the cube table, not `device_states`). **Neither of this slice's specs touches May** (both use June): the 6 added tests shifted worker timing enough to surface a latent collision, which is perturbation, not causation. Fixed by moving `fleet-uptime-report` to **March 2026**, a month nothing else writes — an `afterAll` cleanup cannot help, because the collision is *concurrent*, not leftover. Same class as [#215](./issues/215-shared-fixture-se-zone-leak.md). **Final verification on a frozen tree: 358/358 files, 1,652 passed, 5 skipped, `fleet-uptime-report` green; the only failure is `voucher-controller`'s 2 — the documented #215 baseline.** Process note recorded because it cost real time: **twice this session a spec was edited while a full run was in flight**, invalidating the run and, the first time, producing a phantom `engineers-availability` failure that was chased before being recognised as self-inflicted. The rule the next session should hold: once a verification run starts, the tree is frozen until it reports. | `b82c5af` *(committed 2026-08-13, not on the day of the work — see the 08-13 row)* |

(4) `FleetActivityTrendChart` carried `drop-shadow` glow on all three lines (decoration that blurred near-equal series together) — removed. **One defect was only visible once rendered, not from the code:** that same chart put a device *stock* (~1,000s) and ticket *flow* (~10s) on one axis, pinning two of three series flat — split onto colour-keyed dual axes, and the tooltip drops its total because summing a stock and two flows is meaningless. **Rendering caught three more the compiler could not**: a percent axis emitting duplicate `99% / 99%` ticks (fractional ticks rounding onto the same label), an axis switching notation mid-scale (`14k / 10.5k / 7,000 / 3,500` — compaction was decided per tick, now per axis), and a `ChartLegend` stretched in a `1fr` cell that stranded its counts hundreds of px from their labels. **MUI is used where it earns its place and no further**: `theme/muiTheme.ts` mirrors the two `index.css` token blocks as literals (MUI's `alpha()`/`lighten()` parse the colour string, so a `var(--color-*)` reaches them unresolved and throws), `MuiBridge` feeds it the app's resolved theme, and exactly one component consumes it — `ChartSegmentedControl`, replacing two hand-rolled `aria-pressed` button groups and bringing roving focus they lacked. Radix still owns Dialog/Select/Tabs/Tooltip; no MUI `CssBaseline`. **Measured cost, stated plainly: MUI + Emotion add +121 kB raw / +41 kB gzip for that one control** (built both ways to get the number). RED on all three (`04:00` UTC not `22:30`; recommendation named the dedicated SE not the planner-named one; schedule dated `2026-06-21` not `06-22`) → GREEN via `timeZone: BUSINESS_TIMEZONE` + two `istDate(now)` substitutions; docstring corrected (AC-4). Cron pin asserted **behaviourally** (absolute next fire) per the #204 precedent, not via decorator metadata. Sibling specs `recommender-planner-bias` 2/2 and `critical-assign` 2/2 stay green — their `now` is 11:30 IST, same day either way, which is exactly why they never caught it. **#241 (`removal_reason`):** `removed_by IS NULL` was already auto-recovery's signature (1,092 rows), so nothing recorded *why* a row stopped being live — the distinction #242/#243/#244 each key on and none can recover later. Added nullable `removal_reason` TEXT (not an enum, so later slices add members without a migration; closed vocabulary of 12 in `scheduling/removal-reason.ts`), idempotent backfill, plain `(ticket_id)` index + `soft_states (ticket_id, set_at)`. **Seven writers stamp — five existing, three new.** The three new ones are the real find: device departure, plant deactivation and component-resubmit each closed/unassigned a ticket while leaving its batch row **live**, and every day-plan read filters `removed_at IS NULL` and *not* ticket status — so cancelled and pool-returned tickets kept rendering on SE day plans as work to do (3,310 rows, 20 on ACTIVE schedules). Neither path had a test that looked at the assignment row. **Honest TDD note:** schema + vocabulary + stamps preceded their assertions (no test can reference a column that does not exist), so redness was proven by **stashing the implementation and re-running** — 4 writer reds (`null` vs the expected reason) and 3 leak reds (row still live). AC-2 is asserted **table-wide**, not over the fixture, so any future writer that forgets to stamp fails there. **Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full backend suite **368 files accounted for, 1746 passed / 2 failed / 5 skipped** — both failures `voucher-controller.e2e-spec.ts`, **pre-existing**, proven by stashing every change from this session and re-running (fails identically; fails differently again in isolation, i.e. fixture-state dependent, the #156 class). One #184 worker crash retried and recovered by `run-tests.mjs`. **Dev DB migrated** (`prisma migrate deploy` → `fsm`@5433): 16,382 rows / 7,891 removed measured beforehand, matching the 08-18 audit exactly; after backfill **6,799 `HUMAN_REMOVED` + 1,092 `AUTO_RECOVERY`, 0 removed-without-reason, 0 live-with-reason**, and `EXPLAIN` confirms the per-ticket history read is now an `Index Scan`. **This is not #243** — nothing recycled, unassigned or state-normalised; the cleanup stays HITL and unexecuted, and now has the `DEV_CLEANUP` marker it depends on. `override.service.ts` spans both slices, so its #240 half was staged separately (the #128 `git add -p` precedent); the many pre-existing uncommitted files (admin charts, #238, acting-zone, `SYSTEM-STATE`'s other edits) were kept out of both commits per the handoff. **Next in the block: #242 (blocked only by #243's execution gate) and #250.** | `eb80645` `ada87b8` |

| 2026-08-19 | **#246 DONE — the return date finally has consequences, TDD.** Until this slice the date an SE typed was read by nothing in scheduling: the ticket stayed `FORMALLY_ASSIGNED` on that day's batch, burning a capacity slot, and the next run planned it again as if the vehicle were standing there. Three writes in `fileReport`'s existing transaction close it — live batch row stamped `VEHICLE_UNAVAILABLE` (the #241 member that had no writer, and what makes the window countable by #244 as *reached but unsuccessful*), ticket back to `UNASSIGNED` so something **can** re-plan it, and `deferred_until` so nothing re-plans it **yet**; the last two are a pair, since either alone is a bug (#146's permanent stranding, or "returned to the pool" meaning re-dispatchable within the hour). **Decision 14:** the unit is an IST calendar **day**, so a vehicle back at 4pm today gets *no* deferral — an hour-level wait would invent machinery no reader could honour; `null` is a real answer and is what lets a manager moving the date back onto today **clear** the wait. IST≠UTC for 5h30m daily (19:00Z is already tomorrow IST) — pinned as its own test. **Decision 10:** no horizon, no cap; +90 days pinned end-to-end so nobody later "helpfully" adds a limit. The issue's named `docs/ui/mobile/` VU reference image **does not exist** (third mis-filed Reference in this block, first claiming one that is absent) — corrected in place. | 2026-08-19 | **#244 DONE — Special, derived from the ledger, with one definition serving four surfaces.** The population could not exist before #242: no ticket had ever reached a third assignment attempt. Each of the three definitions is an **artefact**, not an intention — window = one `batch_assignment_tickets` row (the only thing every assignment path writes; SWAP_SE re-points the *batch* so it continues one window, REORDER writes none), *reached* = a `soft_states` row **inside** that window (earliest is the mobile auto-posted VIEWED, so it proves the SE **opened** the ticket and deliberately claims nothing about handset delivery), *success* = a `troubleshooting_submissions` row (the one success writer). Countable = reached ∧ unsubmitted ∧ ended `PLAN_EXPIRED`/`VEHICLE_UNAVAILABLE`; every other reason is excluded because somebody **decided** the attempt should end, so a future reason code is excluded **by default** — the safe direction, since a wrong inclusion fabricates a Special. **No column, no counter, no writer:** a threshold move reclassifies the open book on the next read (both ways), a late submission un-Specials with nothing to undo (no offline queue — a lost submit arrives on re-send), and a verdict read changes **no table count anywhere**, `failure_cycles` included. **One SQL predicate** is evaluated by the queue badge, the `special=true` filter, the count and the Device read — the API spec asserts the filter returns *exactly* the badged ids as an identity, because a queue badging rows its own filter hides is invisible in a unit test of either half. **Two recorded judgement calls the written definition does not settle:** INSTALL excluded (an install can never carry a troubleshooting submission, so without the clause every repeatedly-dispatched install is Special by vacuous truth — "dispatched a lot" wearing the badge), and "no submission" read per-window for countability / per-ticket for the verdict (cannot disagree; more informative in the history). **The ladder exposed a real settings gap rather than needing a second governance service:** `PUT /api/settings/:key` validated **nothing**, while the #238-style reader coerces an illegal stored value to its default — so the settings page would read back the operator's number while the engine used another. `SETTING_VALIDATORS` now refuses at the boundary **with the allowed list** and stores the coerced value. Threshold `special_ticket_attempt_threshold` (2–10, default 3) is OH-owned with **no** co-owner, unlike #238's — nobody feels a classification threshold day to day. **Admin** built to `07-tickets` (SIGNALS column) + `28-tickets-drawer`: `SPECIAL · n` in violet (not REPEAT's orange or ESCALATED's red — three concepts in one colour read as three shades of one alarm), a filter toggle with a **server-side** count (a page count on a chip understates the queue the moment it paginates), and the attempt windows leading the Assignment History tab under a verdict stated against the live threshold — every window listed, withdrawals and the live one included, so the count can be reconciled against the ledger. **Device Detail narrowed deliberately**: that page represents a ticket as a single link, so it gained the chip and left the history one click away rather than duplicating a section onto a one-line panel (the surfacing rule's "do not redesign"). 41 new tests, all red first; **five sensitivity probes** on the derivation (drop reached / widen the window bound / drop the TROUBLESHOOT narrowing / drop OPEN / drop the submission disqualifier) each turned exactly the right test red. Route-ordering pinned (`special-count` before `:id`, else it 404s as an unknown ticket — a browser-only failure). **Query plan measured, not asserted:** `EXPLAIN` on the *dev* DB shows the window read is an `Index Scan` on #241's index; the `soft_states` probe seq-scans because that table holds **17 rows in the whole database**, and pinning a plan against near-empty `fsm_test` would assert whatever the planner does on no data. A fixture bug the suite caught and kept: seeding a settings key outside the registry leaves it description-less for ever, because `seedDefaults` upserts with `update: {}`. **Full backend: 380 files all accounted for after one crash-triggered retry, 1844 passed / 2 failed** — the known pre-existing `voucher-controller` pair. Admin **514/515 with one flake in a different file each run (`se-management`), 4/4 green in isolation**; `vite build` and both typechecks clean. | `e18a3a2` |
| 2026-08-19 | **#242 DONE — the day plan ends, and so does the assignment; Option B steps 1 and 2 in one commit.** Closure wrote a terminal `work_schedules.status` and touched nothing else, so an unworked ticket kept `removed_at NULL` + `FORMALLY_ASSIGNED` for ever and sat in a **double limbo** — invisible to the recommender (`OPEN` + `UNASSIGNED` only) *and* to the SE (every day-plan read needs a *live* schedule). 4,983 tickets were stranded that way and **no ticket on this platform had ever reached a third assignment attempt** (max 2, all via human bulk-unassign), so #244's Special could never fire. `closeZone` now ends those windows — `PLAN_EXPIRED`, `removed_by NULL`, ticket back to `UNASSIGNED` — plus a `RESOLVED_AT_CLOSURE` backstop for a straggler row on an already-resolved ticket, **stamped but never unassigned**. **Three things are pinned rather than claimed.** ① **Order:** the recycle runs *after* the PARTIAL/COMPLETED computation, because that computation asks whether any still-assigned ticket was left unresolved — recycling first removes exactly those rows, so every closing schedule would read `COMPLETED`, a day the SE never finished recorded as finished; moving the call up turns **#147's own** COMPLETED/PARTIAL test red (verified). ② **Set-based, not a fan-out** — a dispatch that finds the zone lock held skips the zone for the whole run, leaving its SEs planless, so this is asserted as a fact about the *data*: every row a zone stamps carries the **identical** `removed_at`, which a per-ticket loop taking its own `new Date()` cannot produce. ③ **`deferred_until` is never written** — a ticket can be recycled *and* still waiting on a vehicle (#246). **A race closed while building:** `OverrideService` takes no advisory lock, so a ZM withdrawing at 04:00 can commit between the method's read and its writes; And it **corrected the severity claim** — every `sla_paused` reader is a reporting/display consumer (`dashboard.service.ts:921`, `ticket-query.service.ts:177`, component receipt, the sweep); dispatch urgency reads `device_states.sla_bucket`, so a stranded pause corrupts downtime accounting, it does **not** suppress the urgency bucket, and the issue no longer says it does. **Three more concrete fixes:** #259 contradicted itself (CONTENDED rows on a refused run that opens no run row — impossible, `DispatchRunZone.runId` needs a parent), now split explicitly into all-held → 409 + zero rows vs. partial → run row + CONTENDED rows; #267 assumed Prisma could read `plants.location`, which is `Unsupported("geometry(Point,4326)")` (`schema.prisma:352`) and invisible to the client → raw `ST_Y/ST_X` prefetch once per zone-run, cached, with NULL geometry → `NOT_AVAILABLE` and an explicit ban on defaulting to `(0,0)`; #268 would have **silently zeroed a live report cube** by renaming `ESCALATION_REQUIRED`, which `system-efficiency-aggregation.service.ts:214-219` counts into `auto_escalations` → keep the value, add `ASSIGNED_DIRECT` only, document the narrowed meaning. #262 gained the interleaving analysis the split needs: **closure is provably safe** (it targets `dateTo < today`, `schedule-closure-scheduler.service.ts:166`, disjoint from today's writes) but **bulk-unassign is not** (same-day, same key), so closure and bulk-unassign now take the #259 zone claim and zone plan-shaping has one owner. **Two decisions the review escalated rather than invented, both since ruled:** **Q-A** — the Plant Cluster Multiplier could not influence selection even under Q1, because `multiplier = isSeed ? 1 : clusterMultiplier` is one value per ticket-plant applied to every candidate, so it cancels out; ruled candidate-specific, derived from the per-SE plant set already implicit in `committedDayLoad`, deliberately same-plant-only so it stays orthogonal to #267's distance. **Q-B** — CRITICAL with every eligible SE at capacity escalates to a manager who may then exceed capacity; **no automatic CRITICAL-only bypass**, so Q2 and Q3 both survive intact. **Order corrected:** #261 before #260 (patience without reaping just waits out a zombie), #259 hard before #262, #265 hard before #268, and **#177/#178 promoted from "adjacent backlog" to hard prerequisites** of #266 and #269/#268 — a selection rewrite belongs over a correct pool, and capacity must be honest before it is displayed or escalated on. #252/#124 folded into #259/#262 rather than built twice. **Sixteen items now in the P8 table; still zero implementation** — no source, migration, data, or cleanup change in this session or the last; #243 remains HITL-gated and unexecuted. | (uncommitted — planning session) |

The TODO predicted exactly this and expected #267's distance to trigger it; clustering got there first. ② **`scoreDegenerate` was falsified by slice 1 without its expression being touched** — it read `distanceFromPrevStopKm === null || weights.distance === 0` ("distance is the only per-SE component, so everything ties and precedence decided"), so on precisely the tickets where clustering separated two candidates and the **score** genuinely decided, the flag still announced that **precedence** had. Worse than a stale field: an operator reading "precedence decided" goes hunting for a coverage explanation that does not exist. Now derived from the spread of the scores actually computed — the only form that stays true as #267 adds another per-candidate term. ③ **Candidates in a losing tier were traced `PASSED` with a score**, as though weighed and lost on merit; they were never scored at all, since the tier is chosen first and the score consulted only inside it → **`TIER_NOT_REACHED`** with a null score. The winner's own `score`, `breakdown` (carrying the clustering contribution Q-A requires it to show) and `tierEvaluated` are added as additive JSON. **Admin shipped in the same slice — and the issue's UI claim was stale**: it says the drawer "renders per-candidate scores (values change, layout doesn't)" when the drawer rendered **no** scores at all. It now shows the winner's and each runner-up's, badges `TIER_NOT_REACHED` **neutral rather than amber** (amber asserts something went wrong for that candidate; nothing did), and the degeneracy copy **no longer blames travel distance** — that stopped being the reason scores can tie, and naming it would send an operator to a setting that is not the cause. **One expectation re-derived, not blind-updated:** `dispatch-transparency`'s runner-up `seB` is MULTI_PLANT while the winner `seA` is DEDICATED, so its tier was never reached — the old test asserted `PASSED` plus `typeof score === 'number'`, i.e. **it was pinning the defect**; title corrected with it. **Verification: backend + admin `tsc` clean; full backend 394 files passed / 3 skipped (397), 1940 passed / 0 failed / 5 skipped, exit 0** — exactly +1 file and +4 tests over the slice-1 baseline, skipped unchanged at 3; **full admin 103 files, 532/532**. **#266 remains PARTIAL:** the three dead seeded weights + their migration, the canonical-ordering pin and the preview-parity assertion are not built.
